        Idea:
Build `megaplan cloud` — a new subpackage at `megaplan/cloud/` that provides a built-in, provider-agnostic cloud runner wired into the existing megaplan CLI. This is sprint 1 of a two-sprint effort; the goal here is operational parity with the existing Railway setup at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`, nothing more.

### What exists today (to be generalised)
The folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:
- `Dockerfile` — Ubuntu 22.04 + pyenv/Python 3.11 + nvm/Node 20 + codex CLI + claude CLI + megaplan from git
- `entrypoint.sh` — idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases→codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver
- `healthserver.py` — tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck
- `railway.toml` — build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy
- `chain.sh` — thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`
- `chain.yaml.example` — chain spec format
- `README.md` — recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands

Hardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases→codex routing, `standard` robustness. Bug to fix: Claude Code CLI is installed in the image but the entrypoint never authenticates it — add an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` auth path.

### Sprint 1 deliverables
1. New subpackage `megaplan/cloud/` parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`, with:
   - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.
   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency — confirm/add.
   - Template renderer that materialises `entrypoint.sh` with spec values at deploy time, so the baked docker image stays generic while the per-deploy entrypoint carries the config.
   - Claude auth path in the rendered entrypoint: if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env is set, run non-interactive auth (investigate what `claude` CLI exposes for this — `claude setup-token` or similar; document whatever you find).
   - Railway provider plugin: thin wrapper over `railway` CLI (`up`, `down`, `ssh`, `variables --set`, `logs`, `status`). Live at `megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py` with a `Provider` abstract base at `megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py` ready for sprint-2 plugins.

2. New argparse subcommands wired into `megaplan/cli.py`:
   - `megaplan cloud init` — scaffold `cloud.yaml` at project root
   - `megaplan cloud build` — docker build the image
   - `megaplan cloud deploy` — render entrypoint, upload vars, `railway up --detach --ci`
   - `megaplan cloud status` — invoke `megaplan status` remotely via provider ssh, return structured JSON (do NOT reinvent a parser — reuse what `megaplan status` already emits)
   - `megaplan cloud attach` — attach to tmux `agent` session
   - `megaplan cloud logs` — tail remote logs
   - `megaplan cloud exec "<cmd>"` — run arbitrary remote command (replaces README one-liners)
   - `megaplan cloud resume` — read remote plan state, dispatch `next_step`
   - `megaplan cloud down` — pause without deleting volume
   - `megaplan cloud destroy` — full teardown

3. Documentation at `docs/cloud.md`: env-var / cloud.yaml reference, symptom→cause→fix table (port from existing README), secret-rotation and cost-monitoring pointers.

4. Tests:
   - Unit: spec loader (happy path + validation errors)
   - Unit: template renderer (all hardcoded strings correctly replaced)
   - Unit: Railway provider with mocked subprocess
   - Smoke: `docker build` on the generated Dockerfile must succeed (CI-runnable; can be skipped on systems without docker with a clear warning)

5. Reigh parity validation (success criterion, not a test to automate): after implementation, the existing `reigh-megaplan-dev` behavior can be reproduced by `megaplan cloud deploy` with a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env vars. The validation itself can be deferred to a follow-up runbook; the code must make it possible.

### Explicit non-goals for sprint 1
- Do NOT ship fly / ssh / local providers — that is sprint 2.
- Do NOT ship `cloud init-plan` bootstrap — sprint 2.
- Do NOT add a `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` — sprint 2.
- Do NOT retire `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` — it keeps running in parallel until sprint 2 migrates reigh fully.

### Constraints (hard requirements)
- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable: if sprint 2 slips, this release is still useful on its own.
- Preserve the idempotency contract of the current entrypoint (repo clone only if missing; codex config always overwritten on boot; megaplan always upgraded on boot).
- Preserve `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` in the generated codex config with an explanatory comment — the container IS the sandbox.
- Preserve the `MEGAPLAN_REF` pinning mechanism, now surfaced as `megaplan.ref` in `cloud.yaml` (defaults to `main`).
- `railway` CLI is an acceptable runtime dependency; document it and fail gracefully with a link if missing.
- Do not break existing megaplan commands — `cloud` is additive.

### References
- Existing source at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` — read everything.
- Target subpackage location: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/`.
- Current CLI wiring pattern: `megaplan/cli.py` uses argparse with `subparsers.add_parser(...)` (see lines ~830-1005).
- Verified launcher for megaplan commands: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan ...`. Bare `python` resolves to 3.8.10 which fails on `NotRequired`; the repo venv has 3.11.

        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.

        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.

        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

```
1052	def _find_megaplan_root(start: Path) -> Path:
1053	    """Walk up from *start* to find the git-root directory containing ``.megaplan/``.
1054	
1055	    Strategy: find the git root first (like ``git rev-parse --show-toplevel``),
1056	    then check if it has a ``.megaplan/`` directory.  This avoids ambiguity when
1057	    nested subdirectories also have their own ``.megaplan/``.  Falls back to the
1058	    nearest ancestor with ``.megaplan/`` if not in a git repo, and finally to
1059	    *start* if nothing is found.
1060	    """
1061	    resolved = start.resolve()
1062	
1063	    # Try git root first — the canonical project root.
1064	    git_root = _find_git_root(resolved)
1065	    if git_root and (git_root / ".megaplan").is_dir():
1066	        return git_root
1067	
1068	    # Fallback: walk up to find nearest .megaplan
1069	    current = resolved
1070	    while True:
1071	        if (current / ".megaplan").is_dir():
1072	            return current
1073	        parent = current.parent
1074	        if parent == current:
1075	            return start
1076	        current = parent
1077	
1078	
1079	def _find_git_root(start: Path) -> Path | None:
1080	    """Walk up to find the directory containing ``.git``."""
1081	    current = start
1082	    while True:
1083	        if (current / ".git").exists():
1084	            return current
1085	        parent = current.parent
1086	        if parent == current:
1087	            return None
1088	        current = parent
1089	
1090
```

```
1	"""Chain driver — run a pipeline of milestone plans with state kept in megaplan.
2	
3	This replaces ad-hoc bash orchestration (`chain.sh`). A YAML spec declares an
4	optional seed plan and an ordered list of milestones; each milestone is
5	initialized from an idea file, then driven to `done` via the same auto-loop
6	entry point used by `megaplan auto`.
7	
8	Plan state stays in megaplan. Bash is no longer responsible for polling or
9	deciding the next step — only for process/container liveness.
10	
11	Spec format (YAML)::
12	
13	    seed:
14	      plan: milestone-m0-from-docs-state-20260415-0217
15	    milestones:
16	      - label: m1
17	        idea: /workspace/ideas/M1-foundation-store.txt
18	        branch: megaplan/m1-foundation-store   # optional, currently informational
19	      - label: m1a
20	        idea: /workspace/ideas/M1a-settings-store.txt
21	    on_failure:
22	      abort: stop_chain          # stop_chain | skip_milestone | retry_milestone
23	    on_escalate:
24	      abort: stop_chain          # stop_chain | skip_milestone | retry_milestone
25	
26	Progress is persisted to ``chain_state.json`` beside the spec so a relaunched
27	process can resume where the previous run left off.
28	"""
29	from __future__ import annotations
30	
31	import argparse
32	import json
33	import subprocess
34	import sys
35	import time
36	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
37	from pathlib import Path
38	from typing import Any
39	
40	try:
41	    import yaml
42	except ImportError as exc:  # pragma: no cover - import guard
43	    raise RuntimeError(
44	        "megaplan chain requires PyYAML. Install with `pip install pyyaml`."
45	    ) from exc
46	
47	from megaplan.auto import (
48	    DEFAULT_MAX_ITERATIONS,
49	    DEFAULT_PHASE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
50	    DEFAULT_POLL_SLEEP_SECONDS,
51	    DEFAULT_STALL_THRESHOLD,
52	    DEFAULT_STATUS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
53	    DriverOutcome,
54	    ESCALATE_ACTIONS,
55	    drive as auto_drive,
56	)
57	from megaplan._core import resolve_plan_dir
58	from megaplan.types import CliError
59	
60	
61	VALID_FAILURE_ACTIONS = ("stop_chain", "skip_milestone", "retry_milestone")
62	TERMINAL_SKIP_STATES = ("done", "aborted", "failed")
63	
64	
65	@dataclass
66	class MilestoneSpec:
67	    label: str
68	    idea: str
69	    branch: str | None = None
70	
71	    @classmethod
72	    def from_dict(cls, raw: dict[str, Any], index: int) -> "MilestoneSpec":
73	        if not isinstance(raw, dict):
74	            raise CliError("invalid_spec", f"milestones[{index}] must be a mapping")
75	        label = raw.get("label")
76	        idea = raw.get("idea")
77	        if not isinstance(label, str) or not label.strip():
78	            raise CliError("invalid_spec", f"milestones[{index}].label is required")
79	        if not isinstance(idea, str) or not idea.strip():
80	            raise CliError("invalid_spec", f"milestones[{index}].idea is required")
```

```
459

483	        state.last_state = outcome.status
484	        save_chain_state(spec_path, state)
485	        decision = _handle_outcome(outcome, spec=spec, writer=writer)
486	
487	        if decision == "stop":
488	            return _result(
489	                "stopped",
490	                state,
491	                events,
492	                reason=f"milestone {milestone.label} ended {outcome.status}",
493	            )
494	        if decision == "retry":
495	            log(f"retrying milestone {milestone.label}")
496	            state.current_plan_name = None  # force re-init next loop
497	            save_chain_state(spec_path, state)
498	            continue
499	        # advance or skip
500	        state.completed.append(
501	            {"label": milestone.label, "plan": plan_name, "status": outcome.status}
502	        )
503	        idx += 1
504	        state.current_milestone_index = idx
505	        state.current_plan_name = None
506	        save_chain_state(spec_path, state)
507	
508	    log("all milestones complete")
509	    return _result("done", state, events)

511	
512	def _result(
513	    status: str, state: ChainState, events: list[dict[str, Any]], *, reason: str = ""
514	) -> dict[str, Any]:
515	    return {
516	        "status": status,
517	        "reason": reason,
518	        "chain_state": state.to_dict(),
519	        "events": events,
520	    }
521	
522	
523	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
524	# CLI plumbing
525	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
526	
527	
528	def build_chain_parser(subparsers: Any) -> None:
529	    chain_parser = subparsers.add_parser(
530	        "chain",
531	        help="Drive a pipeline of milestone plans described by a YAML spec",
532	    )
533	    chain_sub = chain_parser.add_subparsers(dest="chain_action")
534	    # No action == run. `status` is the explicit subcommand.
535	    chain_parser.add_argument(
536	        "--spec",
537	        required=False,
538	        help="Path to the chain spec YAML (required at top-level or on subcommands)",
539	    )
540	    chain_parser.add_argument(
541	        "--no-git-refresh",
542	        action="store_true",
543	        help=(
544	            "Skip the automatic `git checkout main && git pull` that runs "
545	            "before each milestone. Use this on developer checkouts where "
546	            "you do not want chain to stomp on the currently checked-out "
547	            "branch. Default: refresh enabled (preserves CI/orchestrator "
548	            "behavior)."
549	        ),
550	    )
551	
552	    status_parser = chain_sub.add_parser(
553	        "status", help="Show persisted chain progress without driving"
554	    )
555	    status_parser.add_argument("--spec", required=True, help="Path to the chain spec YAML")
556	
557	
558	def run_chain_cli(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
559	    action = getattr(args, "chain_action", None)
560	    spec_arg = getattr(args, "spec", None)
561	    if not spec_arg:
562	        sys.stderr.write("megaplan chain: --spec is required\n")
563	        return 64
564	    spec_path = Path(spec_arg).expanduser().resolve()
565	
566	    if action == "status":
567	        try:
568	            spec = load_spec(spec_path)
569	        except CliError as exc:
570	            return _emit_error(exc)
571	        chain_state = load_chain_state(spec_path)
572	        payload = {
573	            "success": True,
574	            "spec": str(spec_path),
575	            "milestone_count": len(spec.milestones),
576	            "seed_plan": spec.seed_plan,
577	            "chain_state": chain_state.to_dict(),
578	        }
579	        sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
580	        return 0
581	
582	    # Default action: run.
583	    no_git_refresh = bool(getattr(args, "no_git_refresh", False))
584	    try:
585	        result = run_chain(spec_path, root, no_git_refresh=no_git_refresh)
586	    except CliError as exc:
587	        return _emit_error(exc)
588	    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(result, indent=2) + "\n")
589	    if result["status"] == "done":
590	        return 0
591	    return 1
592	
593	
594	def _emit_error(error: CliError) -> int:
595	    payload = {"success": False, "error": error.code, "message": error.message}
596	    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
597	    return error.exit_code or 1
```

I have enough context to produce the plan. Quick summary of what I confirmed:
- `PyYAML>=6.0` is **already** a dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`) — the brief's note to "confirm/add" can be answered.
- Hatchling builds the `megaplan` package wholesale (`pyproject.toml:28-29`), so adding `megaplan/cloud/templates/*` and `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/*` will be packaged by default.
- The CLI follows a clean per-module pattern: `build_<X>_parser(subparsers)` + `run_<X>_cli(root, args)`, dispatched from `cli.py:1126-1145`. `chain.py` is the closest precedent.
- Package resources are loaded via `importlib.resources.files("megaplan").joinpath(...)` (`cli.py:600`).
- `handle_status` already emits structured JSON (`cli.py:353-367`); `cloud status` can reuse this verbatim over SSH.
- I was **blocked** from reading `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` (outside session-allowed paths). The plan is written from the brief, with a Step 0 explicitly to ingest those files first; if any detail diverges, that step is the reconciliation point.

```json
{
  "plan": "# Implementation Plan: `megaplan cloud` — Sprint 1 (provider-agnostic Railway runner)\n\n## Overview\n\nGoal: introduce a new `megaplan/cloud/` subpackage that generalises the working-but-hardcoded Railway deployment at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` into a reusable, spec-driven cloud runner exposed through the existing `megaplan` CLI. Sprint 1 ships only the Railway provider; the abstraction is built so sprint 2 can drop in fly/ssh/local providers without churn.\n\nRepo shape (relevant): the `megaplan` package uses argparse subcommands wired in `megaplan/cli.py` (`build_parser`, `cli.py:826`), with multi-action subcommands defined per-module via `build_<X>_parser(subparsers)` + `run_<X>_cli(root, args)` (see `megaplan/chain.py:528` and dispatch at `megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`). Package data is loaded with `importlib.resources.files(\"megaplan\")` (`megaplan/cli.py:600`). Hatchling packages the whole `megaplan/` tree, so any new resource files (Dockerfile, entrypoint, wrappers, templates) ship automatically. PyYAML is already a runtime dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`).\n\nConstraints that drove design choices:\n- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable.\n- Idempotency contract of the boot script must be preserved (clone-if-missing, codex config always overwritten, megaplan always upgraded).\n- Codex `sandbox_mode = \"danger-full-access\"` retained with explanatory comment.\n- `MEGAPLAN_REF` pin surfaced as `megaplan.ref` (default `main`).\n- Adding `cloud` must not break any existing command.\n- Reigh parity: a `cloud.yaml` matching today's reigh env vars must be able to reproduce the existing deployment.\n\n## Phase 1: Reconcile Source & Scaffold Subpackage\n\n### Step 1: Read the existing reigh deployment (`~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/*`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Read** every file in `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`: `Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh`, `healthserver.py`, `railway.toml`, `chain.sh`, `chain.yaml.example`, `README.md`. (The planning agent did not have read access to that path; the executor must.)\n2. **Catalogue** every hardcoded value (repo URL, workspace path, model, reasoning, email, agent routing, robustness) and every environment variable referenced by `entrypoint.sh`. Map each to a `cloud.yaml` field per the brief's schema.\n3. **Note** the exact mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredoc bodies — these become standalone files in step 3.\n4. **Identify** the exact `claude` CLI auth invocation gap (the brief says \"investigate `claude setup-token` or similar\"). Test what the installed `claude` CLI exposes for non-interactive auth and pick the simplest path. Document it in `docs/cloud.md`.\n5. **Reconcile** any divergence between the brief's described schema and reality before writing code; update the spec schema in step 4 if needed.\n\n### Step 2: Scaffold the subpackage layout (`megaplan/cloud/`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Create** the directory tree:\n   ```\n   megaplan/cloud/\n     __init__.py\n     cli.py              # build_cloud_parser + run_cloud_cli\n     spec.py             # cloud.yaml dataclasses + loader/validator\n     template.py         # entrypoint renderer\n     providers/\n       __init__.py\n       base.py           # Provider ABC\n       railway.py        # Railway implementation\n     templates/\n       Dockerfile\n       entrypoint.sh.tmpl\n       healthserver.py\n       railway.toml.tmpl\n       cloud.yaml.tmpl\n     wrappers/\n       mp-run\n       mp-supervise\n       mp-heartbeat\n   ```\n2. **Confirm** hatchling packages the non-`.py` resources by default. If `pyproject.toml` needs a `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.force-include]` block (e.g. for files without a `.py` extension), add it; otherwise leave it.\n3. **Verify** by building the wheel locally (`./.venv/bin/python -m build --wheel`) and inspecting the wheel contents to confirm `templates/` and `wrappers/` are present.\n\n### Step 3: Port resources verbatim, then parameterise (`megaplan/cloud/templates/`, `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Copy** `Dockerfile`, `healthserver.py`, and `railway.toml` from reigh into `megaplan/cloud/templates/` with no behavioural changes. The Dockerfile stays generic — it bakes the toolchain, not the per-deploy config.\n2. **Copy** `entrypoint.sh` to `megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl` and replace every hardcoded value with a Python `string.Template`-style placeholder (`${REPO_URL}`, `${WORKSPACE_PATH}`, `${CODEX_MODEL}`, `${CODEX_REASONING}`, `${CODEX_EMAIL}`, `${MEGAPLAN_REF}`, `${ROBUSTNESS}`, `${MODE}`, `${IDEA_FILE}`, `${CHAIN_SPEC}`, `${PHASE_AGENT_BLOCK}`, etc.). Keep the explanatory comment for `sandbox_mode = \"danger-full-access\"`.\n3. **Extract** the mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredocs from the original `entrypoint.sh` into standalone files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`. Replace the heredocs in the template with a `cp ${WRAPPERS_DIR}/mp-* /usr/local/bin/ && chmod +x ...` block. The wrappers must be installed into the image at build time (add a `COPY` step to the Dockerfile placing them at a known path).\n4. **Add** a non-interactive Claude auth block to `entrypoint.sh.tmpl`: `if [ -n \"${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY:-}\" ]; then <whatever step 1 found>; fi`. Place it next to the existing codex auth block.\n5. **Add** a `cloud.yaml.tmpl` with sensible defaults filled in and inline comments explaining each section.\n\n### Step 4: Spec loader (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Define** dataclasses mirroring the brief's schema:\n   ```python\n   @dataclass\n   class RepoSpec: url: str; branch: str = \"main\"\n   @dataclass\n   class CodexSpec: model: str; reasoning: str = \"high\"\n   @dataclass\n   class AutoSpec: idea_file: str; robustness: str = \"standard\"\n   @dataclass\n   class ChainSpec: spec: str  # path\n   @dataclass\n   class ResourcesSpec: volume: str | None = None; port: int = 8080\n   @dataclass\n   class MegaplanSpec: ref: str = \"main\"\n   @dataclass\n   class CloudSpec:\n       provider: str\n       repo: RepoSpec\n       agents: dict[str, str]   # {\"default\": \"codex\", optional per-phase overrides}\n       codex: CodexSpec\n       mode: str                # auto | chain | idle\n       megaplan: MegaplanSpec\n       resources: ResourcesSpec\n       secrets: list[str]\n       auto: AutoSpec | None = None\n       chain: ChainSpec | None = None\n   ```\n2. **Implement** `load_spec(path: Path) -> CloudSpec` — open, `yaml.safe_load`, validate, raise `CliError(\"invalid_spec\", ...)` on every missing/malformed field. Mirror the precision of `megaplan/chain.py:71-100`.\n3. **Validate** mode-specific requirements: `mode=auto` ⇒ `auto.idea_file` required; `mode=chain` ⇒ `chain.spec` required; `mode=idle` ⇒ neither required.\n4. **Validate** `agents.default` is in a known set (e.g. `{\"claude\", \"codex\", \"hermes\"}`) and per-phase keys are known phase names.\n5. **Validate** `provider` against the known set — for sprint 1 only `\"railway\"` is permitted; reject others with a `CliError` saying \"sprint 2 will add: fly, ssh, local\".\n\n### Step 5: Template renderer (`megaplan/cloud/template.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Implement** `render_entrypoint(spec: CloudSpec) -> str` using `string.Template.safe_substitute`. Source the `.tmpl` from package resources via `importlib.resources.files(\"megaplan.cloud\").joinpath(\"templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl\").read_text(...)`.\n2. **Build** the per-phase agent override block as a shell snippet (e.g. `megaplan config set phase_agent.<phase> <agent>` lines) when `agents` has entries beyond `default`.\n3. **Implement** `render_railway_toml(spec)` and `materialize_deploy_dir(spec, dest: Path)` that copies the (untemplated) Dockerfile, healthserver, wrappers, and the rendered entrypoint + railway.toml into a deploy staging dir.\n4. **Single source of truth:** the renderer must NOT duplicate placeholder names — define them once at module top so tests can iterate over them.\n\n## Phase 2: Provider Layer\n\n### Step 6: Provider abstract base (`megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Define** an `abc.ABC` `Provider` with the methods sprint-1 commands need:\n   ```python\n   class Provider(ABC):\n       def build(self, deploy_dir: Path) -> int: ...\n       def deploy(self, deploy_dir: Path, *, secrets: dict[str, str]) -> int: ...\n       def ssh_exec(self, command: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess: ...\n       def attach(self, session: str = \"agent\") -> int: ...\n       def logs(self, *, follow: bool = True) -> int: ...\n       def status(self) -> dict: ...\n       def down(self) -> int: ...\n       def destroy(self) -> int: ...\n   ```\n2. **Document** in the docstring that sprint 2 will add `init_plan(self, ...)` and that providers must remain stateless beyond CLI/credential discovery.\n3. **Provide** a small registry helper `get_provider(name: str, spec: CloudSpec) -> Provider` so `cli.py` doesn't import each backend directly.\n\n### Step 7: Railway provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Implement** each ABC method as a thin wrapper around `railway` CLI invocations: `railway up --detach --ci`, `railway down`, `railway ssh ...`, `railway variables --set k=v` (one per secret), `railway logs`, `railway status --json`.\n2. **Detect** the `railway` binary up front; if missing, raise a `CliError` with code `provider_unavailable` and a one-line install hint pointing at https://docs.railway.app/develop/cli.\n3. **`status()`** runs `railway ssh \"./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan status --plan <name>\"` (or `cd /workspace/<repo> && megaplan status`) and returns the parsed JSON. Do not reinvent a parser — `megaplan status` already emits structured JSON via `handle_status` (`cli.py:353`). The plan name comes from a `--plan` arg on `cloud status` or an active-plan lookup.\n4. **`deploy()`** uploads secrets via `railway variables --set` for each name in `spec.secrets`, sourcing values from the local environment. If a named secret is missing locally, error early with a list of missing names. (No silent skipping — secrets matter.)\n5. **No retry/backoff logic** beyond what `railway` already does. Surface non-zero exits as `CliError(\"provider_failed\", stderr)`.\n\n## Phase 3: CLI Wiring\n\n### Step 8: Cloud CLI module (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Implement** `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` modelled on `megaplan/chain.py:528-555`. Top-level `cloud` parser with subparsers: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`. All accept an optional `--cloud-yaml PATH` (defaults to `<root>/cloud.yaml`).\n2. **Implement** `run_cloud_cli(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int` that dispatches to per-action functions. Each action loads the spec (except `init`), constructs a provider via `get_provider`, and calls one method.\n3. **Per-action behaviours:**\n   - **`init`** — copies `cloud.yaml.tmpl` to `<root>/cloud.yaml`. Refuses to overwrite without `--force`.\n   - **`build`** — renders deploy dir, calls `provider.build(deploy_dir)`.\n   - **`deploy`** — renders deploy dir, gathers secret values from env, calls `provider.deploy(...)`.\n   - **`status`** — calls `provider.status()`, prints the JSON it returns. The reviewer/user can pipe to `jq`.\n   - **`attach`** — calls `provider.attach(\"agent\")`. Allow `--session` override (default `agent`, also accepts `heartbeat`).\n   - **`logs`** — calls `provider.logs(follow=True)`. `--no-follow` for one-shot.\n   - **`exec \"<cmd>\"`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec(cmd)`, streams stdout/stderr, returns the remote exit code.\n   - **`resume`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec(\"megaplan status --plan ...\")`, parses `next_step` from the JSON, then `ssh_exec`s the corresponding command. Reuse the `next_step` shape `megaplan auto` already consumes (see `megaplan/auto.py`).\n   - **`down`** — calls `provider.down()`.\n   - **`destroy`** — calls `provider.destroy()`. Prompts for confirmation unless `--yes` is passed.\n4. **Errors:** all `CliError`s flow through `_emit_error` (mirror `megaplan/chain.py:594-597`) so output is consistent with other megaplan commands.\n\n### Step 9: Wire into top-level CLI (`megaplan/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Register** the parser inside `build_parser()` next to the existing `build_chain_parser` registration (`megaplan/cli.py:997-998`):\n   ```python\n   from megaplan.cloud.cli import build_cloud_parser\n   build_cloud_parser(subparsers)\n   ```\n2. **Dispatch** in `main()` next to the `chain` block (`megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`):\n   ```python\n   if args.command == \"cloud\":\n       from megaplan.cloud.cli import run_cloud_cli\n       try:\n           return run_cloud_cli(root, args)\n       except CliError as error:\n           return error_response(error, root=root)\n   ```\n3. **Do NOT** add `\"cloud\"` to `COMMAND_HANDLERS` — it has its own dispatch path, like `auto`/`chain`/`tiebreaker`.\n4. **Keep** all imports lazy/scoped to avoid a perf hit on commands that don't touch cloud.\n\n## Phase 4: Tests, Docs, Smoke Build\n\n### Step 10: Unit tests for spec & renderer (`tests/test_cloud_spec.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **`test_cloud_spec.py`** — happy-path load for each `mode`, plus rejections: missing repo.url, unknown provider, mode=auto without idea_file, mode=chain without chain.spec, unknown agent in `agents.default`, malformed `secrets` list. Mirror `tests/test_chain.py:50-100` style.\n2. **`test_cloud_template.py`** — render with a fully-populated spec and assert (a) every documented placeholder is replaced, (b) no original hardcoded reigh string remains (`banodoco/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4`, `[REDACTED]`), (c) the `sandbox_mode = \"danger-full-access\"` line is preserved, (d) the Claude auth block is present when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is in the secrets list, (e) per-phase agent overrides materialise into the expected `megaplan config set` lines.\n3. **Iterate** over the placeholder constants exported from `template.py` to drive the \"no placeholders left unfilled\" assertion — guards against new placeholders being added without test coverage.\n\n### Step 11: Unit test for the Railway provider with mocked subprocess (`tests/test_cloud_railway.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Patch** `subprocess.run` and assert the exact `railway ...` argv produced by each provider method.\n2. **Cover** `build`, `deploy` (including secret upload sequence), `status` (returns parsed JSON from a mocked stdout), `down`, `destroy`, `ssh_exec`, `attach`, `logs`.\n3. **Cover** the missing-binary path: monkeypatch `shutil.which(\"railway\")` to `None` and assert `CliError(\"provider_unavailable\", ...)`.\n4. **Cover** the missing-secret path: spec lists secret `FOO` but `os.environ` doesn't have it → `CliError` with a list of missing names.\n\n### Step 12: Smoke test for docker build (`tests/test_cloud_docker_build.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Skip** at module import time if `shutil.which(\"docker\") is None` with a clear `pytest.skip(\"docker not available; install Docker to run cloud build smoke test\")`.\n2. **Materialise** a fully-rendered deploy dir into `tmp_path`, then run `docker build --pull=false -t megaplan-cloud-smoke .` against it. Fail the test on non-zero exit.\n3. **Tag** with `@pytest.mark.slow` so CI can opt in/out.\n\n### Step 13: User docs (`docs/cloud.md`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Create** `docs/cloud.md` (the directory does not exist yet — create it). Sections: quick start, `cloud.yaml` field reference, env vars, secret-rotation pointer, cost-monitoring pointer (link to Railway dashboard), and the symptom→cause→fix table ported from the reigh README.\n2. **Document** the exact non-interactive Claude auth path discovered in step 1.\n3. **Document** the `railway` CLI install link and the failure mode if it's missing.\n4. **Brief mention** that sprint 2 will add fly/ssh/local providers and `cloud init-plan` — set expectations, do not over-promise.\n\n## Execution Order\n1. Step 1 first — every later decision (placeholder names, env vars, claude auth, wrapper script bodies) depends on what's actually in reigh.\n2. Steps 2–3 lay down resources before any code references them.\n3. Steps 4–5 (spec + renderer) before steps 6–7 (providers depend on the spec dataclasses).\n4. Steps 8–9 (CLI wiring) after the providers exist.\n5. Tests (10–12) run alongside or after each implementation step they cover; do not save them all for the end.\n6. Docs (13) last so they reflect the shipped behaviour.\n\n## Validation Order\n1. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_spec.py tests/test_cloud_template.py -x` (cheapest, no external deps).\n2. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_railway.py -x` (mocked subprocess, still cheap).\n3. Run the full suite: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -x` to confirm no regression in existing megaplan tests.\n4. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud init` in a scratch dir; confirm `cloud.yaml` is written.\n5. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud build` against the scratch `cloud.yaml`; confirm a Docker image is produced (skip if no docker).\n6. Manual: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud deploy` against a throwaway Railway project mirroring reigh's env vars; confirm the container boots, codex+claude both authenticate, megaplan installs at the pinned ref, tmux sessions start, healthserver answers on `:8080`. Build `megaplan cloud` — a new subpackage at `megaplan/cloud/` that provides a built-in, provider-agnostic cloud runner wired into the existing megaplan CLI. This is sprint 1 of a two-sprint effort; the goal here is operational parity with the existing Railway setup at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`, nothing more.
```

### What exists today (to be generalised)
The folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:
- `Dockerfile` — Ubuntu 22.04 + pyenv/Python 3.11 + nvm/Node 20 + codex CLI + claude CLI + megaplan from git
- `entrypoint.sh` — idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases→codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver
- `healthserver.py` — tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck
- `railway.toml` — build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy
- `chain.sh` — thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`
- `chain.yaml.example` — chain spec format
- `README.md` — recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands

Hardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases→codex routing, `standard` robustness. Bug to fix: Claude Code CLI is installed in the image but the entrypoint never authenticates it — add an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` auth path.

### Sprint 1 deliverables
1. New subpackage `megaplan/cloud/` parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`, with:
   - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.
   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency — confirm/add.
   - Template renderer that materialises `entrypoint.sh` with spec values at deploy time, so the baked docker image stays generic while the per-deploy entrypoint carries the config.
   - Claude auth path in the rendered entrypoint: if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env is set, run non-interactive auth (investigate what `claude` CLI exposes for this — `claude setup-token` or similar; document whatever you find).
   - Railway provider plugin: thin wrapper over `railway` CLI (`up`, `down`, `ssh`, `variables --set`, `logs`, `status`). Live at `megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py` with a `Provider` abstract base at `megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py` ready for sprint-2 plugins.

2. New argparse subcommands wired into `megaplan/cli.py`:
   - `megaplan cloud init` — scaffold `cloud.yaml` at project root
   - `megaplan cloud build` — docker build the image
   - `megaplan cloud deploy` — render entrypoint, upload vars, `railway up --detach --ci`
   - `megaplan cloud status` — invoke `megaplan status` remotely via provider ssh, return structured JSON (do NOT reinvent a parser — reuse what `megaplan status` already emits)
   - `megaplan cloud attach` — attach to tmux `agent` session
   - `megaplan cloud logs` — tail remote logs
   - `megaplan cloud exec "<cmd>"` — run arbitrary remote command (replaces README one-liners)
   - `megaplan cloud resume` — read remote plan state, dispatch `next_step`
   - `megaplan cloud down` — pause without deleting volume

The validation itself can be deferred to a follow-up runbook; the code must make it possible.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: `megaplan cloud` — Sprint 1 (provider-agnostic Railway runner)

## Overview

Goal: introduce a new `megaplan/cloud/` subpackage that generalises the working-but-hardcoded Railway deployment at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` into a reusable, spec-driven cloud runner exposed through the existing `megaplan` CLI. Sprint 1 ships only the Railway provider; the abstraction is built so sprint 2 can drop in fly/ssh/local providers without churn.

Repo shape (relevant): the `megaplan` package uses argparse subcommands wired in `megaplan/cli.py` (`build_parser`, `cli.py:826`), with multi-action subcommands defined per-module via `build_<X>_parser(subparsers)` + `run_<X>_cli(root, args)` (see `megaplan/chain.py:528` and dispatch at `megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`). Package data is loaded with `importlib.resources.files("megaplan")` (`megaplan/cli.py:600`). Hatchling packages the whole `megaplan/` tree, so any new resource files (Dockerfile, entrypoint, wrappers, templates) ship automatically. PyYAML is already a runtime dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`).

Constraints that drove design choices:
- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable.
- Idempotency contract of the boot script must be preserved (clone-if-missing, codex config always overwritten, megaplan always upgraded).
- Codex `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` retained with explanatory comment.
- `MEGAPLAN_REF` pin surfaced as `megaplan.ref` (default `main`).
- Adding `cloud` must not break any existing command.
- Reigh parity: a `cloud.yaml` matching today's reigh env vars must be able to reproduce the existing deployment.

## Phase 1: Reconcile Source & Scaffold Subpackage

### Step 1: Read the existing reigh deployment (`~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/*`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** every file in `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`: `Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh`, `healthserver.py`, `railway.toml`, `chain.sh`, `chain.yaml.example`, `README.md`. (The planning agent did not have read access to that path; the executor must.)
2. **Catalogue** every hardcoded value (repo URL, workspace path, model, reasoning, email, agent routing, robustness) and every environment variable referenced by `entrypoint.sh`. Map each to a `cloud.yaml` field per the brief's schema.
3. **Note** the exact mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredoc bodies — these become standalone files in step 3.
4. **Identify** the exact `claude` CLI auth invocation gap (the brief says "investigate `claude setup-token` or similar"). Test what the installed `claude` CLI exposes for non-interactive auth and pick the simplest path. Document it in `docs/cloud.md`.
5. **Reconcile** any divergence between the brief's described schema and reality before writing code; update the spec schema in step 4 if needed.

### Step 2: Scaffold the subpackage layout (`megaplan/cloud/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** the directory tree:
   ```
   megaplan/cloud/
     __init__.py
     cli.py              # build_cloud_parser + run_cloud_cli
     spec.py             # cloud.yaml dataclasses + loader/validator
     template.py         # entrypoint renderer
     providers/
       __init__.py
       base.py           # Provider ABC
       railway.py        # Railway implementation
     templates/
       Dockerfile
       entrypoint.sh.tmpl
       healthserver.py
       railway.toml.tmpl
       cloud.yaml.tmpl
     wrappers/
       mp-run
       mp-supervise
       mp-heartbeat
   ```
2. **Confirm** hatchling packages the non-`.py` resources by default. If `pyproject.toml` needs a `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.force-include]` block (e.g. for files without a `.py` extension), add it; otherwise leave it.
3. **Verify** by building the wheel locally (`./.venv/bin/python -m build --wheel`) and inspecting the wheel contents to confirm `templates/` and `wrappers/` are present.

### Step 3: Port resources verbatim, then parameterise (`megaplan/cloud/templates/`, `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Copy** `Dockerfile`, `healthserver.py`, and `railway.toml` from reigh into `megaplan/cloud/templates/` with no behavioural changes. The Dockerfile stays generic — it bakes the toolchain, not the per-deploy config.
2. **Copy** `entrypoint.sh` to `megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl` and replace every hardcoded value with a Python `string.Template`-style placeholder (`${REPO_URL}`, `${WORKSPACE_PATH}`, `${CODEX_MODEL}`, `${CODEX_REASONING}`, `${CODEX_EMAIL}`, `${MEGAPLAN_REF}`, `${ROBUSTNESS}`, `${MODE}`, `${IDEA_FILE}`, `${CHAIN_SPEC}`, `${PHASE_AGENT_BLOCK}`, etc.). Keep the explanatory comment for `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`.
3. **Extract** the mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredocs from the original `entrypoint.sh` into standalone files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`. Replace the heredocs in the template with a `cp ${WRAPPERS_DIR}/mp-* /usr/local/bin/ && chmod +x ...` block. The wrappers must be installed into the image at build time (add a `COPY` step to the Dockerfile placing them at a known path).
4. **Add** a non-interactive Claude auth block to `entrypoint.sh.tmpl`: `if [ -n "${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY:-}" ]; then <whatever step 1 found>; fi`. Place it next to the existing codex auth block.
5. **Add** a `cloud.yaml.tmpl` with sensible defaults filled in and inline comments explaining each section.

### Step 4: Spec loader (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** dataclasses mirroring the brief's schema:
   ```python
   @dataclass
   class RepoSpec: url: str; branch: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class CodexSpec: model: str; reasoning: str = "high"
   @dataclass
   class AutoSpec: idea_file: str; robustness: str = "standard"
   @dataclass
   class ChainSpec: spec: str  # path
   @dataclass
   class ResourcesSpec: volume: str | None = None; port: int = 8080
   @dataclass
   class MegaplanSpec: ref: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class CloudSpec:
       provider: str
       repo: RepoSpec
       agents: dict[str, str]   # {"default": "codex", optional per-phase overrides}
       codex: CodexSpec
       mode: str                # auto | chain | idle
       megaplan: MegaplanSpec
       resources: ResourcesSpec
       secrets: list[str]
       auto: AutoSpec | None = None
       chain: ChainSpec | None = None
   ```
2. **Implement** `load_spec(path: Path) -> CloudSpec` — open, `yaml.safe_load`, validate, raise `CliError("invalid_spec", ...)` on every missing/malformed field. Mirror the precision of `megaplan/chain.py:71-100`.
3. **Validate** mode-specific requirements: `mode=auto` ⇒ `auto.idea_file` required; `mode=chain` ⇒ `chain.spec` required; `mode=idle` ⇒ neither required.
4. **Validate** `agents.default` is in a known set (e.g. `{"claude", "codex", "hermes"}`) and per-phase keys are known phase names.
5. **Validate** `provider` against the known set — for sprint 1 only `"railway"` is permitted; reject others with a `CliError` saying "sprint 2 will add: fly, ssh, local".

### Step 5: Template renderer (`megaplan/cloud/template.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `render_entrypoint(spec: CloudSpec) -> str` using `string.Template.safe_substitute`. Source the `.tmpl` from package resources via `importlib.resources.files("megaplan.cloud").joinpath("templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl").read_text(...)`.
2. **Build** the per-phase agent override block as a shell snippet (e.g. `megaplan config set phase_agent.<phase> <agent>` lines) when `agents` has entries beyond `default`.
3. **Implement** `render_railway_toml(spec)` and `materialize_deploy_dir(spec, dest: Path)` that copies the (untemplated) Dockerfile, healthserver, wrappers, and the rendered entrypoint + railway.toml into a deploy staging dir.
4. **Single source of truth:** the renderer must NOT duplicate placeholder names — define them once at module top so tests can iterate over them.

## Phase 2: Provider Layer

### Step 6: Provider abstract base (`megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Define** an `abc.ABC` `Provider` with the methods sprint-1 commands need:
   ```python
   class Provider(ABC):
       def build(self, deploy_dir: Path) -> int: ...
       def deploy(self, deploy_dir: Path, *, secrets: dict[str, str]) -> int: ...
       def ssh_exec(self, command: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess: ...
       def attach(self, session: str = "agent") -> int: ...
       def logs(self, *, follow: bool = True) -> int: ...
       def status(self) -> dict: ...
       def down(self) -> int: ...
       def destroy(self) -> int: ...
   ```
2. **Document** in the docstring that sprint 2 will add `init_plan(self, ...)` and that providers must remain stateless beyond CLI/credential discovery.
3. **Provide** a small registry helper `get_provider(name: str, spec: CloudSpec) -> Provider` so `cli.py` doesn't import each backend directly.

### Step 7: Railway provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** each ABC method as a thin wrapper around `railway` CLI invocations: `railway up --detach --ci`, `railway down`, `railway ssh ...`, `railway variables --set k=v` (one per secret), `railway logs`, `railway status --json`.
2. **Detect** the `railway` binary up front; if missing, raise a `CliError` with code `provider_unavailable` and a one-line install hint pointing at https://docs.railway.app/develop/cli.
3. **`status()`** runs `railway ssh "./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan status --plan <name>"` (or `cd /workspace/<repo> && megaplan status`) and returns the parsed JSON. Do not reinvent a parser — `megaplan status` already emits structured JSON via `handle_status` (`cli.py:353`). The plan name comes from a `--plan` arg on `cloud status` or an active-plan lookup.
4. **`deploy()`** uploads secrets via `railway variables --set` for each name in `spec.secrets`, sourcing values from the local environment. If a named secret is missing locally, error early with a list of missing names. (No silent skipping — secrets matter.)
5. **No retry/backoff logic** beyond what `railway` already does. Surface non-zero exits as `CliError("provider_failed", stderr)`.

## Phase 3: CLI Wiring

### Step 8: Cloud CLI module (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` modelled on `megaplan/chain.py:528-555`. Top-level `cloud` parser with subparsers: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`. All accept an optional `--cloud-yaml PATH` (defaults to `<root>/cloud.yaml`).
2. **Implement** `run_cloud_cli(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int` that dispatches to per-action functions. Each action loads the spec (except `init`), constructs a provider via `get_provider`, and calls one method.
3. **Per-action behaviours:**
   - **`init`** — copies `cloud.yaml.tmpl` to `<root>/cloud.yaml`. Refuses to overwrite without `--force`.
   - **`build`** — renders deploy dir, calls `provider.build(deploy_dir)`.
   - **`deploy`** — renders deploy dir, gathers secret values from env, calls `provider.deploy(...)`.
   - **`status`** — calls `provider.status()`, prints the JSON it returns. The reviewer/user can pipe to `jq`.
   - **`attach`** — calls `provider.attach("agent")`. Allow `--session` override (default `agent`, also accepts `heartbeat`).
   - **`logs`** — calls `provider.logs(follow=True)`. `--no-follow` for one-shot.
   - **`exec "<cmd>"`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec(cmd)`, streams stdout/stderr, returns the remote exit code.
   - **`resume`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec("megaplan status --plan ...")`, parses `next_step` from the JSON, then `ssh_exec`s the corresponding command. Reuse the `next_step` shape `megaplan auto` already consumes (see `megaplan/auto.py`).
   - **`down`** — calls `provider.down()`.
   - **`destroy`** — calls `provider.destroy()`. Prompts for confirmation unless `--yes` is passed.
4. **Errors:** all `CliError`s flow through `_emit_error` (mirror `megaplan/chain.py:594-597`) so output is consistent with other megaplan commands.

### Step 9: Wire into top-level CLI (`megaplan/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Register** the parser inside `build_parser()` next to the existing `build_chain_parser` registration (`megaplan/cli.py:997-998`):
   ```python
   from megaplan.cloud.cli import build_cloud_parser
   build_cloud_parser(subparsers)
   ```
2. **Dispatch** in `main()` next to the `chain` block (`megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`):
   ```python
   if args.command == "cloud":
       from megaplan.cloud.cli import run_cloud_cli
       try:
           return run_cloud_cli(root, args)
       except CliError as error:
           return error_response(error, root=root)
   ```
3. **Do NOT** add `"cloud"` to `COMMAND_HANDLERS` — it has its own dispatch path, like `auto`/`chain`/`tiebreaker`.
4. **Keep** all imports lazy/scoped to avoid a perf hit on commands that don't touch cloud.

## Phase 4: Tests, Docs, Smoke Build

### Step 10: Unit tests for spec & renderer (`tests/test_cloud_spec.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`test_cloud_spec.py`** — happy-path load for each `mode`, plus rejections: missing repo.url, unknown provider, mode=auto without idea_file, mode=chain without chain.spec, unknown agent in `agents.default`, malformed `secrets` list. Mirror `tests/test_chain.py:50-100` style.
2. **`test_cloud_template.py`** — render with a fully-populated spec and assert (a) every documented placeholder is replaced, (b) no original hardcoded reigh string remains (`banodoco/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4`, `[REDACTED]`), (c) the `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` line is preserved, (d) the Claude auth block is present when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is in the secrets list, (e) per-phase agent overrides materialise into the expected `megaplan config set` lines.
3. **Iterate** over the placeholder constants exported from `template.py` to drive the "no placeholders left unfilled" assertion — guards against new placeholders being added without test coverage.

### Step 11: Unit test for the Railway provider with mocked subprocess (`tests/test_cloud_railway.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Patch** `subprocess.run` and assert the exact `railway ...` argv produced by each provider method.
2. **Cover** `build`, `deploy` (including secret upload sequence), `status` (returns parsed JSON from a mocked stdout), `down`, `destroy`, `ssh_exec`, `attach`, `logs`.
3. **Cover** the missing-binary path: monkeypatch `shutil.which("railway")` to `None` and assert `CliError("provider_unavailable", ...)`.
4. **Cover** the missing-secret path: spec lists secret `FOO` but `os.environ` doesn't have it → `CliError` with a list of missing names.

### Step 12: Smoke test for docker build (`tests/test_cloud_docker_build.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Skip** at module import time if `shutil.which("docker") is None` with a clear `pytest.skip("docker not available; install Docker to run cloud build smoke test")`.
2. **Materialise** a fully-rendered deploy dir into `tmp_path`, then run `docker build --pull=false -t megaplan-cloud-smoke .` against it. Fail the test on non-zero exit.
3. **Tag** with `@pytest.mark.slow` so CI can opt in/out.

### Step 13: User docs (`docs/cloud.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `docs/cloud.md` (the directory does not exist yet — create it). Sections: quick start, `cloud.yaml` field reference, env vars, secret-rotation pointer, cost-monitoring pointer (link to Railway dashboard), and the symptom→cause→fix table ported from the reigh README.
2. **Document** the exact non-interactive Claude auth path discovered in step 1.
3. **Document** the `railway` CLI install link and the failure mode if it's missing.
4. **Brief mention** that sprint 2 will add fly/ssh/local providers and `cloud init-plan` — set expectations, do not over-promise.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 first — every later decision (placeholder names, env vars, claude auth, wrapper script bodies) depends on what's actually in reigh.
2. Steps 2–3 lay down resources before any code references them.
3. Steps 4–5 (spec + renderer) before steps 6–7 (providers depend on the spec dataclasses).
4. Steps 8–9 (CLI wiring) after the providers exist.
5. Tests (10–12) run alongside or after each implementation step they cover; do not save them all for the end.
6. Docs (13) last so they reflect the shipped behaviour.

## Validation Order
1. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_spec.py tests/test_cloud_template.py -x` (cheapest, no external deps).
2. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_railway.py -x` (mocked subprocess, still cheap).
3. Run the full suite: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -x` to confirm no regression in existing megaplan tests.
4. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud init` in a scratch dir; confirm `cloud.yaml` is written.
5. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud build` against the scratch `cloud.yaml`; confirm a Docker image is produced (skip if no docker).
6. Manual: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud deploy` against a throwaway Railway project mirroring reigh's env vars; confirm the container boots, codex+claude both authenticate, megaplan installs at the pinned ref, tmux sessions start, healthserver answers on `:8080`. Document this run as the parity-validation success criterion artifact.

  "rationale": "The plan is architecturally sound (subpackage layout, spec/template/provider split, additive CLI wiring) but has several concrete API-contract mismatches with the current codebase that would produce broken code if executed as written.   "settled_decisions": [
    {
      "id": "SD-001",
      "decision": "Subpackage layout `megaplan/cloud/{cli,spec,template,providers/{base,railway},templates/,wrappers/}` with Provider ABC + static `{\"railway\": RailwayProvider}` registry is correct; sprint-2 providers plug in via the same base class.",
      "rationale": "Matches the existing `megaplan/chain.py` / `megaplan/loop/` pattern and satisfies the sprint-1 shippability constraint without over-designing for sprint-2 providers."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-002",
      "decision": "Dockerfile stays generic (bakes toolchain only); per-deploy config flows through a rendered `entrypoint.sh` materialized at deploy time plus Railway env vars.",
      "rationale": "Preserves image cacheability and the existing idempotency contract \u2014 the rendered entrypoint carries the spec values, not an image rebuild."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-003",
      "decision": "Secret values are sourced from the local environment at deploy time using names in `spec.secrets`; missing values fail fast with a list of names (no silent skip).",
      "rationale": "Explicit user preference in the brief and matches the reigh workflow's `railway variables --set` pattern."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-004",
      "decision": "`cloud destroy` requires interactive confirmation unless `--yes` is passed; `cloud status` auto-discovers the active plan when `--plan` is omitted by passing `None` to the existing `load_plan` helper.",
      "rationale": "User answered both in the notes (question 4 and 5); no need to re-litigate."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-005",
      "decision": "Placeholder syntax in templates is Python `string.Template` (`${NAME}`) sourced via `importlib.resources.files`; hatchling's default file inclusion is assumed sufficient and verified by inspecting the built wheel.",
      "rationale": "Avoids a Jinja dependency and keeps the template directly readable as shell/TOML."
    }
  ],

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "Build `megaplan cloud` \u2014 a new subpackage at `megaplan/cloud/` that provides a built-in, provider-agnostic cloud runner wired into the existing megaplan CLI. This is sprint 1 of a two-sprint effort; the goal here is operational parity with the existing Railway setup at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`, nothing more.\n\n### What exists today (to be generalised)\nThe folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:\n- `Dockerfile` \u2014 Ubuntu 22.04 + pyenv/Python 3.11 + nvm/Node 20 + codex CLI + claude CLI + megaplan from git\n- `entrypoint.sh` \u2014 idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases\u2192codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver\n- `healthserver.py` \u2014 tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck\n- `railway.toml` \u2014 build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy\n- `chain.sh` \u2014 thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`\n- `chain.yaml.example` \u2014 chain spec format\n- `README.md` \u2014 recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands\n\nHardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases\u2192codex routing, `standard` robustness. Bug to fix: Claude Code CLI is installed in the image but the entrypoint never authenticates it \u2014 add an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` auth path.\n\n### Sprint 1 deliverables\n1. New subpackage `megaplan/cloud/` parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`, with:\n   - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.\n   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency \u2014 confirm/add.\n   - Template renderer that materialises `entrypoint.sh` with spec values at deploy time, so the baked docker image stays generic while the per-deploy entrypoint carries the config.\n   - Claude auth path in the rendered entrypoint: if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env is set, run non-interactive auth (investigate what `claude` CLI exposes for this \u2014 `claude setup-token` or similar; document whatever you find).\n   - Railway provider plugin: thin wrapper over `railway` CLI (`up`, `down`, `ssh`, `variables --set`, `logs`, `status`). Live at `megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py` with a `Provider` abstract base at `megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py` ready for sprint-2 plugins.\n\n2. New argparse subcommands wired into `megaplan/cli.py`:\n   - `megaplan cloud init` \u2014 scaffold `cloud.yaml` at project root\n   - `megaplan cloud build` \u2014 docker build the image\n   - `megaplan cloud deploy` \u2014 render entrypoint, upload vars, `railway up --detach --ci`\n   - `megaplan cloud status` \u2014 invoke `megaplan status` remotely via provider ssh, return structured JSON (do NOT reinvent a parser \u2014 reuse what `megaplan status` already emits)\n   - `megaplan cloud attach` \u2014 attach to tmux `agent` session\n   - `megaplan cloud logs` \u2014 tail remote logs\n   - `megaplan cloud exec \"<cmd>\"` \u2014 run arbitrary remote command (replaces README one-liners)\n   - `megaplan cloud resume` \u2014 read remote plan state, dispatch `next_step`\n   - `megaplan cloud down` \u2014 pause without deleting volume\n   - `megaplan cloud destroy` \u2014 full teardown\n\n3. Documentation at `docs/cloud.md`: env-var / cloud.yaml reference, symptom\u2192cause\u2192fix table (port from existing README), secret-rotation and cost-monitoring pointers.\n\n4. Tests:\n   - Unit: spec loader (happy path + validation errors)\n   - Unit: template renderer (all hardcoded strings correctly replaced)\n   - Unit: Railway provider with mocked subprocess\n   - Smoke: `docker build` on the generated Dockerfile must succeed (CI-runnable; can be skipped on systems without docker with a clear warning)\n\n5. Reigh parity validation (success criterion, not a test to automate): after implementation, the existing `reigh-megaplan-dev` behavior can be reproduced by `megaplan cloud deploy` with a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env vars. The validation itself can be deferred to a follow-up runbook; the code must make it possible.\n\n### Explicit non-goals for sprint 1\n- Do NOT ship fly / ssh / local providers \u2014 that is sprint 2.\n- Do NOT ship `cloud init-plan` bootstrap \u2014 sprint 2.\n- Do NOT add a `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` \u2014 sprint 2.\n- Do NOT retire `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` \u2014 it keeps running in parallel until sprint 2 migrates reigh fully.\n\n### Constraints (hard requirements)\n- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable: if sprint 2 slips, this release is still useful on its own.\n- Preserve the idempotency contract of the current entrypoint (repo clone only if missing; codex config always overwritten on boot; megaplan always upgraded on boot).\n- Preserve `sandbox_mode = \"danger-full-access\"` in the generated codex config with an explanatory comment \u2014 the container IS the sandbox.\n- Preserve the `MEGAPLAN_REF` pinning mechanism, now surfaced as `megaplan.ref` in `cloud.yaml` (defaults to `main`).\n- `railway` CLI is an acceptable runtime dependency; document it and fail gracefully with a link if missing.\n- Do not break existing megaplan commands \u2014 `cloud` is additive.\n\n### References\n- Existing source at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` \u2014 read everything.\n- Target subpackage location: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/`.\n- Current CLI wiring pattern: `megaplan/cli.py` uses argparse with `subparsers.add_parser(...)` (see lines ~830-1005).\n- Verified launcher for megaplan commands: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan ...`.         Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

```
124	def _has_valid_next(status: dict[str, Any], action: str) -> bool:
125	    return action in (status.get("valid_next") or [])

127	
128	def _phase_command(next_step: str) -> list[str]:
129	    """Translate a `next_step` from status into the CLI args that run it.
130	
131	    Most phases are one-to-one: next_step == command. Execute adds the
132	    destructive + user-approved flags because auto-mode implies both.
133	    """
134	    if next_step == "execute":
135	        return ["execute", "--confirm-destructive", "--user-approved"]
136	    return [next_step]
137	
138	
139	def _resolve_plan_dir(plan: str, cwd: Path | None) -> Path | None:
140	    """Best-effort resolution of ``.megaplan/plans/<plan>`` near ``cwd``.
141	
142	    Walks up parents of ``cwd`` looking for a ``.megaplan/plans/<plan>``
143	    directory, matching how ``megaplan status`` resolves plans. Returns
144	    ``None`` if the plan dir can't be located — callers should treat that
145	    as "no review marker available" and fall back to the plain stall-count.
146	    """
147	    base = (cwd or Path.cwd()).resolve()
148	    for candidate in (base, *base.parents):
149	        plan_dir = candidate / ".megaplan" / "plans" / plan
150	        if (plan_dir / "state.json").exists():
151	            return plan_dir
152	    return None
153	
154	
155	def _get_review_marker(plan_dir: Path | None) -> float | None:
156	    """Return a monotonically-advancing marker for the current review cycle.
157	
158	    Uses ``review.json`` mtime — each completed review phase rewrites the
159	    file, so the mtime bumps once per review cycle. This is race-free
```

### What exists today (to be generalised)
The folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:

- `entrypoint.sh` — idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases→codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver
- `healthserver.py` — tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck
- `railway.toml` — build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy
- `chain.sh` — thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`
- `chain.yaml.example` — chain spec format
- `README.md` — recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands

Hardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases→codex routing, `standard` robustness.    - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.
   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency — confirm/add.
