        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: `megaplan cloud` — Sprint 1 (provider-agnostic Railway runner)

## Overview

Goal: introduce a new `megaplan/cloud/` subpackage that generalises the working-but-hardcoded Railway deployment at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` into a reusable, spec-driven cloud runner exposed through the existing `megaplan` CLI. Sprint 1 ships only the Railway provider; the abstraction is built so sprint 2 can drop in fly/ssh/local providers without churn.

Repo shape (relevant): the `megaplan` package uses argparse subcommands wired in `megaplan/cli.py` (`build_parser`, `cli.py:826`), with multi-action subcommands defined per-module via `build_<X>_parser(subparsers)` + `run_<X>_cli(root, args)` (see `megaplan/chain.py:528` and dispatch at `megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`). Package data is loaded with `importlib.resources.files("megaplan")` (`megaplan/cli.py:600`). Hatchling packages the whole `megaplan/` tree, so any new resource files (Dockerfile, entrypoint, wrappers, templates) ship automatically. PyYAML is already a runtime dependency (`pyproject.toml:22`).

Hard contracts the plan must respect (verified against the codebase, fixing prior-iteration mistakes):
- **Remote command name**: the deployed image installs `megaplan` globally (reigh `Dockerfile:61-63`); README operations use bare `megaplan` inside `/workspace/<repo>`. The cloud layer must use `megaplan ...`, NOT `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan` — there is no `.venv` in the container.
- **Config namespace**: `megaplan config set` only accepts `agents.<step>` keys, where `<step>` is validated against `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (`megaplan/cli.py:758-777`, `megaplan/types.py:296-309`). There is no `phase_agent.*` namespace. The renderer must emit `agents.<step>` lines and reject unknown step names by importing `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING`.
- **`next_step` translation**: `megaplan/auto.py:128-136` (`_phase_command`) is the canonical translator and adds `--confirm-destructive --user-approved` for `execute`. `cloud resume` must reuse this helper, not stringify `next_step` directly.
- **Active-plan resolution**: `handle_status` (`cli.py:353-367`) calls `load_plan(root, args.plan)` which already auto-resolves when `args.plan is None`. The cloud layer must not duplicate this — `cloud status` and `cloud resume` simply pass `--plan <name>` through if the user provided one and omit the flag otherwise.
- **Railway service/session targeting**: the existing reigh workflow uses `railway --service agent variables --set ...` and `railway ssh --session agent` (per the reigh README). The Railway provider must thread service + session identity through every CLI call, sourced from a `railway:` block in `cloud.yaml`.

Other constraints:
- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable.
- Idempotency contract of the boot script preserved (clone-if-missing, codex config always overwritten, megaplan always upgraded).
- Codex `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` retained with explanatory comment.
- `MEGAPLAN_REF` pin surfaced as `megaplan.ref` (default `main`).
- Adding `cloud` must not break any existing command.
- Reigh parity: a `cloud.yaml` matching today's reigh env vars must be able to reproduce the existing deployment.

## Phase 1: Reconcile Source & Scaffold Subpackage

### Step 0: Vendor reigh source as reference (`megaplan/cloud/_reference/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** the executor can read `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`. If readable, proceed; if sandboxed (the planning agent was), copy the entire directory tree into `megaplan/cloud/_reference/` (gitignored) so subsequent steps have ground truth without permission churn.
2. **Document** in a brief `megaplan/cloud/_reference/README.md` (or top-of-file comment) that the contents are a frozen reference snapshot, not the runtime templates.

### Step 1: Read every file in the reigh deployment
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** every file in `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` (or the vendored mirror from Step 0): `Dockerfile`, `entrypoint.sh`, `healthserver.py`, `railway.toml`, `chain.sh`, `chain.yaml.example`, `README.md`, `BRIEFING_RULES.md`, every file under `ideas/`, every top-level `megaplan-idea*.txt`, plus anything else present. Do not pre-filter — the brief says "every file" and it means every file.
2. **Catalogue** every hardcoded value (repo URL, workspace path, model, reasoning, email, agent routing, robustness) and every environment variable referenced by `entrypoint.sh`. Map each to a `cloud.yaml` field.
3. **Note** the exact Railway service name (`agent`) and tmux session names (`agent`, `heartbeat`) used throughout the README — these become defaults in the `railway:` block of `cloud.yaml`.
4. **Note** the exact mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredoc bodies — these become standalone files in step 3.
5. **Identify** the exact `claude` CLI auth invocation. Test what the installed `claude` CLI exposes for non-interactive auth (`claude setup-token`, `claude auth ...`, env-var-driven, etc.). Per user note #3: if no non-interactive path exists, the rendered entrypoint must emit a clear warning and continue (do NOT block deploy). Document the chosen path in `docs/cloud.md`.

### Step 2: Scaffold the subpackage layout (`megaplan/cloud/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** the directory tree:
   ```
   megaplan/cloud/
     __init__.py
     cli.py              # build_cloud_parser + run_cloud_cli
     spec.py             # cloud.yaml dataclasses + loader/validator
     template.py         # entrypoint renderer
     resume.py           # imports _phase_command from megaplan.auto and exposes a remote-friendly wrapper
     providers/
       __init__.py
       base.py           # Provider ABC
       railway.py        # Railway implementation
     templates/
       Dockerfile
       entrypoint.sh.tmpl
       healthserver.py
       railway.toml.tmpl
       cloud.yaml.tmpl
     wrappers/
       mp-run
       mp-supervise
       mp-heartbeat
   ```
2. **Confirm** hatchling packages the non-`.py` resources by default. If `pyproject.toml` needs a `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.force-include]` block, add it; otherwise leave it.
3. **Verify** by building the wheel locally (`./.venv/bin/python -m build --wheel`) and inspecting wheel contents.

### Step 3: Port resources verbatim, then parameterise (`megaplan/cloud/templates/`, `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Copy** `Dockerfile`, `healthserver.py`, and `railway.toml` from reigh into `megaplan/cloud/templates/` with no behavioural changes. The Dockerfile bakes the toolchain only; megaplan is installed globally inside the image (preserve the existing `pip install` line so `megaplan` resolves on the container PATH — do NOT switch to a project-local `.venv`).
2. **Copy** `entrypoint.sh` to `megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl` and replace every hardcoded value with a Python `string.Template` placeholder (`${REPO_URL}`, `${REPO_BRANCH}`, `${WORKSPACE_PATH}`, `${CODEX_MODEL}`, `${CODEX_REASONING}`, `${CODEX_EMAIL}`, `${MEGAPLAN_REF}`, `${ROBUSTNESS}`, `${MODE}`, `${IDEA_FILE}`, `${CHAIN_SPEC}`, `${AGENT_ROUTING_BLOCK}`, `${CLAUDE_AUTH_BLOCK}`, etc.). Keep the explanatory comment for `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`.
3. **Extract** the mp-run / mp-supervise / mp-heartbeat heredocs from the original `entrypoint.sh` into standalone files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`. Add a `COPY megaplan/cloud/wrappers/* /usr/local/bin/` (or equivalent) step to the Dockerfile, plus a `chmod +x`. The template no longer carries heredocs.
4. **Render** the Claude auth block as the result of step 1's investigation. Shape:
   ```
   if [ -n "${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY:-}" ]; then
       <discovered command> || echo "[entrypoint] WARNING: claude auth failed; continuing"
   else
       echo "[entrypoint] ANTHROPIC_API_KEY not set; skipping claude auth"
   fi
   ```
   If no non-interactive path exists at all, the block becomes a single `echo "[entrypoint] WARNING: claude CLI has no non-interactive auth; tools relying on claude will fail"` — never `exit 1`.
5. **Add** a `cloud.yaml.tmpl` with sensible defaults filled in and inline comments explaining each section.

### Step 4: Spec loader (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Define** dataclasses mirroring the schema. Provider-specific knobs live under a per-provider nested block so the Provider ABC stays clean for sprint 2:
   ```python
   @dataclass
   class RepoSpec: url: str; branch: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class CodexSpec: model: str = "gpt-5.4"; reasoning: str = "high"
   @dataclass
   class AutoSpec: idea_file: str; robustness: str = "standard"
   @dataclass
   class ChainSubSpec: spec: str
   @dataclass
   class ResourcesSpec: volume: str | None = None; port: int = 8080
   @dataclass
   class MegaplanSpec: ref: str = "main"
   @dataclass
   class RailwaySpec: service: str = "agent"; session: str = "agent"; project: str | None = None
   @dataclass
   class CloudSpec:
       provider: str                     # "railway" only in sprint 1
       repo: RepoSpec
       agents: dict[str, str]            # keys validated against DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING + "default"
       codex: CodexSpec
       mode: str                         # "auto" | "chain" | "idle"
       megaplan: MegaplanSpec
       resources: ResourcesSpec
       secrets: list[str]
       auto: AutoSpec | None = None
       chain: ChainSubSpec | None = None
       railway: RailwaySpec | None = None  # required when provider == "railway"
   ```
2. **Implement** `load_spec(path: Path) -> CloudSpec` — open, `yaml.safe_load`, validate, raise `CliError("invalid_spec", ...)` on every missing/malformed field. Mirror `megaplan/chain.py:71-100`.
3. **Validate** mode-specific requirements: `mode=auto` ⇒ `auto.idea_file` required; `mode=chain` ⇒ `chain.spec` required; `mode=idle` ⇒ neither required.
4. **Validate** `agents` keys: `default` is permitted; every other key MUST be in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (imported from `megaplan.types`). This eliminates drift — when the routing system grows in `megaplan.types`, cloud.yaml automatically accepts the new step. Reject unknown keys with `CliError("invalid_spec", f"agents.{key} not in DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING: {sorted(DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING)}")`.
5. **Validate** every value in `agents` is in `KNOWN_AGENTS` (also imported from `megaplan.types`).
6. **Validate** `provider`: only `"railway"` is permitted in sprint 1; reject others with `CliError("invalid_spec", "sprint 2 will add: fly, ssh, local")`.
7. **Validate** that when `provider == "railway"`, the `railway:` block exists (use defaults if omitted, but the dataclass instance must be present after load).

### Step 5: Template renderer (`megaplan/cloud/template.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `render_entrypoint(spec: CloudSpec) -> str` using `string.Template.safe_substitute`. Source the `.tmpl` from package resources via `importlib.resources.files("megaplan.cloud").joinpath("templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl").read_text(...)`.
2. **Build** the agent-routing block. For each entry in `spec.agents`:
   - `default` → `megaplan config set agents.<step> <default_agent>` for every `<step>` in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (preserves the "all phases → codex" semantics reigh has today).
   - Per-step keys → `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` (overrides default for that step only).
   The output is a sequence of valid `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` shell lines — no `phase_agent.*`, no fabricated namespace.
3. **Implement** `render_railway_toml(spec)` and `materialize_deploy_dir(spec, dest: Path)` that copies the Dockerfile, healthserver, wrappers, and the rendered entrypoint + railway.toml into a deploy staging dir. The deploy staging dir is what the provider hands to `railway up`.
4. **Single source of truth**: define placeholder names as a module-level constant (e.g. `PLACEHOLDERS = ("REPO_URL", "REPO_BRANCH", ...)`) so tests can iterate over them and assert "no placeholder remains unfilled".

## Phase 2: Provider Layer

### Step 6: Provider abstract base (`megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Define** an `abc.ABC` `Provider` with the methods sprint-1 commands need. Keep the surface minimal so sprint 2 providers can implement it without leaking Railway-specific concepts:
   ```python
   class Provider(ABC):
       def build(self, deploy_dir: Path) -> int: ...
       def deploy(self, deploy_dir: Path, *, secrets: dict[str, str]) -> int: ...
       def ssh_exec(self, command: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess: ...
       def attach(self) -> int: ...                  # provider decides session/target
       def logs(self, *, follow: bool = True) -> int: ...
       def status_payload(self, *, plan: str | None) -> dict: ...  # remote `megaplan status [--plan ...]`
       def down(self) -> int: ...
       def destroy(self) -> int: ...
   ```
2. **Document** in the docstring that sprint 2 will add `init_plan(self, ...)` and that providers must remain stateless beyond CLI/credential discovery.
3. **Provide** `get_provider(name: str, spec: CloudSpec) -> Provider` so `cli.py` doesn't import each backend directly. Static dict for sprint 1: `{"railway": RailwayProvider}`.

### Step 7: Railway provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Construct** with the `RailwaySpec` (`service`, `session`, optional `project`) so every method targets the right service explicitly.
2. **Implement** each ABC method as a thin wrapper around `railway` CLI:
   - `build`: `docker build -t <image-tag> <deploy_dir>` (pre-Railway local build, optional).
   - `deploy`: render service-targeted `railway up --service <service> --detach --ci` from the deploy dir; preceded by per-secret `railway variables --service <service> --set NAME=VALUE` calls.
   - `ssh_exec(cmd)`: `railway ssh --service <service> --session <session> -- <cmd>` — the session arg matches the existing reigh README.
   - `attach`: `railway ssh --service <service> --session <session>` (interactive). The cloud CLI step accepts `--session` to override the default for `attach` only.
   - `logs`: `railway logs --service <service>` (with `--follow` if requested).
   - `status_payload(plan)`: build remote command as `cd ${WORKSPACE_PATH} && megaplan status` plus `--plan <plan>` if `plan is not None`. Stream stdout, parse JSON, return dict. Use bare `megaplan` (globally installed in the image) — no `.venv`, no `python -m`.
   - `down`: `railway down --service <service>` (or service-targeted equivalent).
   - `destroy`: full teardown using whatever `railway` exposes; the safety prompt lives in the CLI layer, not here.
3. **Detect** the `railway` binary up front via `shutil.which("railway")`; if missing, raise `CliError("provider_unavailable", "Install: https://docs.railway.app/develop/cli")`.
4. **`deploy()` secrets**: source values from `os.environ` for each name in `spec.secrets`. If any are missing locally, raise `CliError("missing_secrets", f"Missing env vars: {missing}")` BEFORE making any railway calls. No silent skip.
5. **No retry/backoff** beyond what `railway` already does. Surface non-zero exits as `CliError("provider_failed", stderr)`.

## Phase 3: CLI Wiring

### Step 8: Cloud CLI module (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` modelled on `megaplan/chain.py:528-555`. Top-level `cloud` parser with subparsers: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`. All accept an optional `--cloud-yaml PATH` (defaults to `<root>/cloud.yaml`).
2. **Implement** `run_cloud_cli(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int` that dispatches to per-action functions. Each action loads the spec (except `init`), constructs a provider via `get_provider`, and calls one method.
3. **Per-action behaviours:**
   - **`init`** — copies `cloud.yaml.tmpl` to `<root>/cloud.yaml`. Refuses to overwrite without `--force`.
   - **`build`** — renders deploy dir, calls `provider.build(deploy_dir)`.
   - **`deploy`** — renders deploy dir, gathers secret values from env, calls `provider.deploy(...)`.
   - **`status`** — accepts optional `--plan NAME`; calls `provider.status_payload(plan=args.plan)` and prints the JSON it returns. Auto-discovery happens server-side: when `--plan` is omitted, the remote `megaplan status` is invoked with no `--plan` flag, and `load_plan(root, None)` in `_core` resolves the active plan. No client-side discovery, no extra remote round-trips.
   - **`attach`** — calls `provider.attach()`. `--session NAME` overrides the spec default for this single invocation.
   - **`logs`** — calls `provider.logs(follow=True)`. `--no-follow` for one-shot.
   - **`exec "<cmd>"`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec(cmd)`, streams stdout/stderr, returns the remote exit code.
   - **`resume`** — accepts optional `--plan NAME`. Steps:
     1. Call `provider.status_payload(plan=args.plan)`; read `next_step` from the result.
     2. Translate via the local helper: `from megaplan.auto import _phase_command; argv = _phase_command(next_step)`. This guarantees the `execute` special case (`execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved`) is honored — same translator the local `auto` driver uses, so behaviour cannot drift.
     3. Build the remote command: `cd ${WORKSPACE_PATH} && megaplan ` + shell-quoted `argv`, plus `--plan <plan>` if the user specified one.
     4. Hand to `provider.ssh_exec(...)`.
   - **`down`** — calls `provider.down()`.
   - **`destroy`** — interactive confirmation by default; `--yes` skips. (Per user note #5.) On confirmation, calls `provider.destroy()`.
4. **Errors:** all `CliError`s flow through `_emit_error` (mirror `megaplan/chain.py:594-597`) so output is consistent with other megaplan commands.

### Step 9: Wire into top-level CLI (`megaplan/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Register** the parser inside `build_parser()` next to the existing `build_chain_parser` registration (`megaplan/cli.py:997-998`):
   ```python
   from megaplan.cloud.cli import build_cloud_parser
   build_cloud_parser(subparsers)
   ```
2. **Dispatch** in `main()` next to the `chain` block (`megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`):
   ```python
   if args.command == "cloud":
       from megaplan.cloud.cli import run_cloud_cli
       try:
           return run_cloud_cli(root, args)
       except CliError as error:
           return error_response(error, root=root)
   ```
3. **Do NOT** add `"cloud"` to `COMMAND_HANDLERS` — it has its own dispatch path, like `auto`/`chain`/`tiebreaker`.
4. **Keep** all imports lazy/scoped to avoid a perf hit on commands that don't touch cloud.

## Phase 4: Tests, Docs, Smoke Build

### Step 10: Unit tests for spec & renderer (`tests/test_cloud_spec.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`test_cloud_spec.py`** — happy-path load for each `mode`, plus rejections: missing `repo.url`, unknown provider, `mode=auto` without `idea_file`, `mode=chain` without `chain.spec`, an `agents` key that isn't `default` AND isn't in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (assert the error names the actual valid set), an unknown agent value (not in `KNOWN_AGENTS`), malformed `secrets` list, and (for `provider: railway`) a missing `railway:` block being filled with defaults that match reigh (`service: agent`, `session: agent`).
2. **`test_cloud_template.py`** — render with a fully-populated spec and assert: (a) every documented placeholder is replaced (iterate over `template.PLACEHOLDERS`); (b) no original reigh hardcoded string remains (`banodoco/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4`, `[REDACTED]`); (c) `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"` line is preserved; (d) Claude auth block is present and gated on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (see step 3); (e) the agent-routing block emits ONLY `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` lines (no `phase_agent.*`); (f) for a per-step override, exactly one `agents.<step>` line is emitted with the override agent and the rest match the default.

### Step 11: Unit test for the Railway provider with mocked subprocess (`tests/test_cloud_railway.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Patch** `subprocess.run` (and `shutil.which`) and assert exact `railway ...` argv produced by each provider method.
2. **Cover** that every railway invocation includes `--service <service>` from the spec and that `ssh_exec`/`attach` include `--session <session>`.
3. **Cover** `build`, `deploy` (including the per-secret upload sequence with `--service`), `status_payload(plan=None)` and `status_payload(plan="P")` (assert remote command shapes), `down`, `destroy`, `ssh_exec`, `attach`, `logs`.
4. **Assert** the remote command for `status_payload` is `cd <workspace> && megaplan status` (plus `--plan ...` if provided) — no `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan`, no `python -m`.
5. **Cover** the missing-binary path: `shutil.which("railway") -> None` ⇒ `CliError("provider_unavailable", ...)`.
6. **Cover** the missing-secret path: spec lists secret `FOO` but `os.environ` doesn't have it → `CliError` with the missing names.

### Step 12: Unit test for `cloud resume` translation (`tests/test_cloud_resume.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Mock** `provider.status_payload` to return a dict with `next_step == "execute"`. Assert the cloud-resume action calls `provider.ssh_exec` with a remote command containing `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved`. (This is the regression guard for FLAG-003.)
2. **Cover** at least one non-execute step (e.g. `next_step == "review"`) to verify the bare-name path also works.
3. **Assert** that `--plan <name>` is appended when the user passed `--plan`, and omitted when they didn't.

### Step 13: Smoke test for docker build (`tests/test_cloud_docker_build.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Skip** at module import if `shutil.which("docker") is None` with a clear `pytest.skip(...)` message.
2. **Materialise** a fully-rendered deploy dir into `tmp_path`, run `docker build -t megaplan-cloud-smoke .` against it. Fail on non-zero exit.
3. **Mark** with `@pytest.mark.slow` so CI can opt in/out.

### Step 14: User docs (`docs/cloud.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `docs/cloud.md` (the directory does not exist yet — create it). Sections: quick start, `cloud.yaml` field reference (including the `railway:` nested block with `service`/`session` and the rationale that other providers will get their own nested block), env vars, secret-rotation pointer, cost-monitoring pointer (Railway dashboard link), the symptom→cause→fix table ported from the reigh README.
2. **Document** the exact non-interactive Claude auth path discovered in step 1 — and explicitly call out the warning-not-fatal behaviour when no path exists.
3. **Document** the `railway` CLI install link and the failure mode if it's missing.
4. **Brief mention** that sprint 2 will add fly/ssh/local providers and `cloud init-plan` — set expectations, do not over-promise.

## Execution Order
1. Steps 0–1 first — vendoring (if needed) and full read of the reigh dir feeds every later decision.
2. Steps 2–3 lay down resources before any code references them.
3. Steps 4–5 (spec + renderer) before steps 6–7 (providers depend on the spec dataclasses).
4. Steps 8–9 (CLI wiring) after the providers exist.
5. Tests (10–13) run alongside or after each implementation step they cover; do not save them all for the end.
6. Docs (14) last so they reflect the shipped behaviour.

## Validation Order
1. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_spec.py tests/test_cloud_template.py tests/test_cloud_resume.py -x` (cheapest, no external deps).
2. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_railway.py -x` (mocked subprocess, still cheap).
3. Run the full suite: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -x` to confirm no regression in existing megaplan tests.
4. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud init` in a scratch dir; confirm `cloud.yaml` is written.
5. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud build` against the scratch `cloud.yaml`; confirm a Docker image is produced (skip if no docker).
6. Manual: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud deploy` against a throwaway Railway project mirroring reigh's env vars; confirm the container boots, codex authenticates, claude auth either succeeds or warns (never blocks), megaplan installs at the pinned ref, tmux sessions start, healthserver answers on `:8080`. `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud status` returns parsed JSON, `cloud resume` on an `execute`-state plan dispatches `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved` over SSH. Document this run as the parity-validation success criterion artifact.

    "`cloud status` and `cloud resume` perform server-side plan auto-discovery for free: omitting `--plan` from the remote `megaplan status` invocation lets `load_plan(root, None)` resolve the active plan. No client-side discovery is implemented.",
    "Provider-specific config (Railway service/session/project) lives under a `railway:` nested block in `cloud.yaml` so sprint-2 providers can introduce their own nested blocks (`fly:`, `ssh:`, `local:`) without touching the abstract base.",
    "`cloud destroy` requires interactive confirmation unless `--yes` is passed (per user note #5); the prompt lives in the CLI layer, not the provider.",
    "If the `claude` CLI exposes no non-interactive auth path, the rendered entrypoint emits a warning and continues \u2014 it never `exit 1`s the boot sequence (per user note #3).",
    "If the executor is sandboxed out of `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` (the planning agent was), Step 0 vendors the directory tree into `megaplan/cloud/_reference/` (gitignored) so subsequent steps have a stable source of truth.",
    "The placeholder syntax in `entrypoint.sh.tmpl` is `${NAME}` (Python `string.Template`) sourced via `importlib.resources.files`."
  ],

Specific fixes the v3 plan must make: (1) **Workspace path** \u2014 add a `workspace: str` field (either on `RepoSpec` with default `/workspace/app`, or as a top-level `workspace.path` \u2014 either is fine, just declare it) and thread it into `render_entrypoint` (the `safe.directory`, `REPO_DIR`, tmux `-c`, clone destination all use it), `RailwayProvider.status_payload` (`cd <workspace> && megaplan status`), and `resume` remote command construction. Covers issue_hints, correctness-1, callers-1, FLAG-005 \u2014 all the same underlying fix. (2) **Volume teardown** \u2014 extend `Provider.destroy(self, *, volume: str | None = None) -> int` and in `RailwayProvider.destroy`, after `railway down --service <service>`, if `spec.resources.volume` is set call `railway volume delete <volume>` (guarded by the CLI-layer interactive confirm that already exists). Add a unit test that asserts the two-call sequence when volume is set and the single-call path when it's `None`. Covers correctness-2, callers-2, FLAG-006, all_locations-1. (3) **`railway.project`** \u2014 either make the deploy step call `railway link --project <project>` once up front when `spec.railway.project is not None` (and assert it in the provider tests), or drop the field from `RailwaySpec` entirely. Don't keep a dead knob. (4) **`_reference/` plumbing** \u2014 explicitly add `megaplan/cloud/_reference/` to `.gitignore` in Step 0 and, when Step 2 touches `pyproject.toml` for hatchling force-include, exclude `_reference/` from the wheel via `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.exclude]`.   "signals": {
    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "Build `megaplan cloud` \u2014 a new subpackage at `megaplan/cloud/` that provides a built-in, provider-agnostic cloud runner wired into the existing megaplan CLI. This is sprint 1 of a two-sprint effort; the goal here is operational parity with the existing Railway setup at `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`, nothing more.\n\n### What exists today (to be generalised)\nThe folder `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` contains a working but hardcoded Railway deployment. Read every file there during prep. Key pieces:\n- `Dockerfile` \u2014 Ubuntu 22.04 + pyenv/Python 3.11 + nvm/Node 20 + codex CLI + claude CLI + megaplan from git\n- `entrypoint.sh` \u2014 idempotent boot: codex auth (OPENAI_API_KEY), codex `config.toml` (model=gpt-5.4, reasoning=high, sandbox_mode=danger-full-access, approval_policy=never), megaplan install/upgrade from `MEGAPLAN_REF`, megaplan config (all phases\u2192codex, auto_approve=true, robustness=standard), repo clone, installs `mp-run`/`mp-supervise`/`mp-heartbeat` wrapper scripts (currently embedded as heredocs), starts tmux sessions `agent` and `heartbeat`, starts healthserver\n- `healthserver.py` \u2014 tiny HTTP server on :8080 for Railway healthcheck\n- `railway.toml` \u2014 build/deploy config, healthcheck, restart policy\n- `chain.sh` \u2014 thin shim around `megaplan chain --spec /workspace/chain.yaml`\n- `chain.yaml.example` \u2014 chain spec format\n- `README.md` \u2014 recipes for status/attach/resume/abort/restart/teardown that need to become subcommands\n\nHardcoded things to parameterise: `banodoco/reigh-app.git`, `/workspace/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4` model, `high` reasoning, `[REDACTED]` email, all-phases\u2192codex routing, `standard` robustness. Bug to fix: Claude Code CLI is installed in the image but the entrypoint never authenticates it \u2014 add an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` auth path.\n\n### Sprint 1 deliverables\n1. New subpackage `megaplan/cloud/` parallel to `megaplan/loop/` and `megaplan/chain.py`, with:\n   - Package resources: ported Dockerfile, entrypoint.sh, healthserver.py. Extract the mp-run/mp-supervise/mp-heartbeat bash scripts (currently embedded as heredocs in entrypoint.sh) into their own files under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`.\n   - Spec loader for `cloud.yaml` (schema covers at minimum: `provider`, `repo.url`/`branch`, `agents.default` + optional per-phase map, `codex.model` + `reasoning`, `mode` = auto|chain|idle, `auto.idea_file`, `auto.robustness`, `chain.spec`, `megaplan.ref`, `resources.volume`/`port`, `secrets` name list). PyYAML is not yet a dependency \u2014 confirm/add.\n   - Template renderer that materialises `entrypoint.sh` with spec values at deploy time, so the baked docker image stays generic while the per-deploy entrypoint carries the config.\n   - Claude auth path in the rendered entrypoint: if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` env is set, run non-interactive auth (investigate what `claude` CLI exposes for this \u2014 `claude setup-token` or similar; document whatever you find).\n   - Railway provider plugin: thin wrapper over `railway` CLI (`up`, `down`, `ssh`, `variables --set`, `logs`, `status`). Live at `megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py` with a `Provider` abstract base at `megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py` ready for sprint-2 plugins.\n\n2. New argparse subcommands wired into `megaplan/cli.py`:\n   - `megaplan cloud init` \u2014 scaffold `cloud.yaml` at project root\n   - `megaplan cloud build` \u2014 docker build the image\n   - `megaplan cloud deploy` \u2014 render entrypoint, upload vars, `railway up --detach --ci`\n   - `megaplan cloud status` \u2014 invoke `megaplan status` remotely via provider ssh, return structured JSON (do NOT reinvent a parser \u2014 reuse what `megaplan status` already emits)\n   - `megaplan cloud attach` \u2014 attach to tmux `agent` session\n   - `megaplan cloud logs` \u2014 tail remote logs\n   - `megaplan cloud exec \"<cmd>\"` \u2014 run arbitrary remote command (replaces README one-liners)\n   - `megaplan cloud resume` \u2014 read remote plan state, dispatch `next_step`\n   - `megaplan cloud down` \u2014 pause without deleting volume\n   - `megaplan cloud destroy` \u2014 full teardown\n\n3. Documentation at `docs/cloud.md`: env-var / cloud.yaml reference, symptom\u2192cause\u2192fix table (port from existing README), secret-rotation and cost-monitoring pointers.\n\n4. Tests:\n   - Unit: spec loader (happy path + validation errors)\n   - Unit: template renderer (all hardcoded strings correctly replaced)\n   - Unit: Railway provider with mocked subprocess\n   - Smoke: `docker build` on the generated Dockerfile must succeed (CI-runnable; can be skipped on systems without docker with a clear warning)\n\n5. Reigh parity validation (success criterion, not a test to automate): after implementation, the existing `reigh-megaplan-dev` behavior can be reproduced by `megaplan cloud deploy` with a `cloud.yaml` matching reigh's current env vars. The validation itself can be deferred to a follow-up runbook; the code must make it possible.\n\n### Explicit non-goals for sprint 1\n- Do NOT ship fly / ssh / local providers \u2014 that is sprint 2.\n- Do NOT ship `cloud init-plan` bootstrap \u2014 sprint 2.\n- Do NOT add a `toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` \u2014 sprint 2.\n- Do NOT retire `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` \u2014 it keeps running in parallel until sprint 2 migrates reigh fully.\n\n### Constraints (hard requirements)\n- Sprint 1 must be independently shippable: if sprint 2 slips, this release is still useful on its own.\n- Preserve the idempotency contract of the current entrypoint (repo clone only if missing; codex config always overwritten on boot; megaplan always upgraded on boot).\n- Preserve `sandbox_mode = \"danger-full-access\"` in the generated codex config with an explanatory comment \u2014 the container IS the sandbox.\n- Preserve the `MEGAPLAN_REF` pinning mechanism, now surfaced as `megaplan.ref` in `cloud.yaml` (defaults to `main`).\n- `railway` CLI is an acceptable runtime dependency; document it and fail gracefully with a link if missing.\n- Do not break existing megaplan commands \u2014 `cloud` is additive.\n\n### References\n- Existing source at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` \u2014 read everything.\n- Target subpackage location: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/`.\n- Current CLI wiring pattern: `megaplan/cli.py` uses argparse with `subparsers.add_parser(...)` (see lines ~830-1005).\n- Verified launcher for megaplan commands: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan ...`. Bare `python` resolves to 3.8.10 which fails on `NotRequired`; the repo venv has 3.11.",
    "significant_flags": 10,
    "unresolved_flags": [
      {
        "id": "issue_hints",
        "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: Issue hints: the brief explicitly calls out `/workspace/reigh-app` as a hardcoded value that must be parameterized, and the revised plan now references `${WORKSPACE_PATH}` in the entrypoint template plus `cd ${WORKSPACE_PATH}` in remote status/resume commands. However, the revised `CloudSpec` still has no field that actually carries the workspace path, so one of the named hard requirements remains under-specified even after the revision.",

      {
        "id": "correctness-1",
        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Cloud config: the revised plan still does not provide a schema field for the repo checkout path even though reigh hardcodes `REPO_DIR=/workspace/reigh-app`, sets git `safe.directory` to that path, starts tmux in that directory, and the README's status/resume commands all `cd` there. Because `render_entrypoint()`, `status_payload()`, and `resume` now all depend on `WORKSPACE_PATH`, the lack of a declared source of truth would force the executor to invent a derivation rule that is not specified in the plan.",
        "category": "correctness",

        "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Workspace-path callers remain under-served.     "resolved_flags": [
      {
        "id": "FLAG-001",
        "concern": "Cloud runtime: remote `status` is planned around `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan`, but the deployed container installs megaplan globally and the existing Railway workflow uses plain `megaplan` inside `/workspace/reigh-app`.",
        "resolution": "Addressed every open significant flag without redesigning the architecture. Concrete corrections: (1) remote commands now use bare `megaplan` (image installs it globally) instead of `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan`; (2) renderer emits `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` validated against `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` \u2014 fabricated `phase_agent.*` namespace is gone; (3) new `cloud resume` step explicitly imports and reuses `megaplan.auto._phase_command()` so the `execute` special case (`--confirm-destructive --user-approved`) cannot silently break, with a new dedicated test (`tests/test_cloud_resume.py`); (4) added a `railway:` nested block in `cloud.yaml` carrying `service`/`session`/`project`, threaded through every railway invocation, kept under a per-provider namespace so sprint-2 providers stay clean; (5) Step 1 now reads every file in the reigh dir including `BRIEFING_RULES.md`, `ideas/`, `megaplan-idea*.txt`; (6) dropped the duplicate remote active-plan lookup \u2014 `cloud status`/`cloud resume` simply pass `--plan` through (or omit) and let `load_plan(root, None)` resolve server-side; (7) phase validation now imports `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` from `megaplan.types` instead of hand-maintaining a phase set, removing drift risk. Added a Step 0 vendoring path for sandboxed executors (per orchestrator warning), and committed the rendered Claude-auth block to \"warn, do not block\" semantics (per user note #3). Added one new success criterion for the resume-execute translation."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-002",

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

The `repo` block now carries the `workspace` path (the field that addresses the workspace-path flags); `resources.volume` is wired in step 7's `destroy`; `railway.project` is wired in step 7's `deploy`:\n   ```python\n   @dataclass\n   class RepoSpec:\n       url: str\n       branch: str = \"main\"\n       workspace: str = \"/workspace/app\"   # absolute container path: clone dest, tmux cwd, README cd target\n   @dataclass\n   class CodexSpec: model: str = \"gpt-5.4\"; reasoning: str = \"high\"\n   @dataclass\n   class AutoSpec: idea_file: str; robustness: str = \"standard\"\n   @dataclass\n   class ChainSubSpec: spec: str\n   @dataclass\n   class ResourcesSpec:\n       volume: str | None = None           # Railway volume name; if set, `cloud destroy` deletes it after `down`\n       port: int = 8080\n   @dataclass\n   class MegaplanSpec: ref: str = \"main\"\n   @dataclass\n   class RailwaySpec:\n       service: str = \"agent\"\n       session: str = \"agent\"\n       project: str | None = None          # if set, `cloud deploy` runs `railway link --project <project>` first\n   @dataclass\n   class CloudSpec:\n       provider: str\n       repo: RepoSpec\n       agents: dict[str, str]              # keys validated against DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING + \"default\"\n       codex: CodexSpec\n       mode: str                           # \"auto\" | \"chain\" | \"idle\"\n       megaplan: MegaplanSpec\n       resources: ResourcesSpec\n       secrets: list[str]\n       auto: AutoSpec | None = None\n       chain: ChainSubSpec | None = None\n       railway: RailwaySpec | None = None  # required when provider == \"railway\"; loader fills with defaults\n   ```\n2. **Implement** `load_spec(path: Path) -> CloudSpec` — open, `yaml.safe_load`, validate, raise `CliError(\"invalid_spec\", ...)` on every missing/malformed field. Mirror `megaplan/chain.py:71-100`.\n3. **Validate** mode-specific requirements: `mode=auto` ⇒ `auto.idea_file` required; `mode=chain` ⇒ `chain.spec` required; `mode=idle` ⇒ neither required.\n4. **Validate** `agents` keys: `default` permitted; every other key MUST be in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (imported from `megaplan.types`). Reject unknown keys with `CliError(\"invalid_spec\", f\"agents.{key} not in DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING: {sorted(DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING)}\")`.\n5. **Validate** every value in `agents` is in `KNOWN_AGENTS`.\n6. **Validate** `provider`: only `\"railway\"` permitted in sprint 1; reject others with `CliError(\"invalid_spec\", \"sprint 2 will add: fly, ssh, local\")`.\n7. **Validate** `repo.workspace` is an absolute POSIX path (starts with `/`); reject otherwise.\n8. **Fill** `RailwaySpec` with defaults if the `railway:` block is omitted, but always materialise the dataclass when `provider == \"railway\"`.\n\n### Step 5: Template renderer (`megaplan/cloud/template.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Implement** `render_entrypoint(spec: CloudSpec) -> str` using `string.Template.safe_substitute`. Source the `.tmpl` from package resources via `importlib.resources.files(\"megaplan.cloud\").joinpath(\"templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl\").read_text(...)`. The substitution map MUST include `WORKSPACE_PATH=spec.repo.workspace` so every reference in the template (safe.directory, REPO_DIR, clone dest, tmux `-c`) resolves to the spec value.\n2. **Build** the agent-routing block. For each entry in `spec.agents`:\n   - `default` → `megaplan config set agents.<step> <default_agent>` for every `<step>` in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING`.\n   - Per-step keys → `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` (overrides default for that step).\n   Output is shell lines only — no `phase_agent.*`, no fabricated namespace.\n3. **Implement** `render_railway_toml(spec)` and `materialize_deploy_dir(spec, dest: Path)` that copies the Dockerfile, healthserver, wrappers, and the rendered entrypoint + railway.toml into a deploy staging dir.\n4. **Single source of truth**: define placeholder names as a module-level tuple `PLACEHOLDERS = (\"REPO_URL\", \"REPO_BRANCH\", \"WORKSPACE_PATH\", ...)` so tests iterate over them and assert \"no placeholder remains unfilled\".\n\n## Phase 2: Provider Layer\n\n### Step 6: Provider abstract base (`megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Define** an `abc.ABC` `Provider`. Note the new `volume` parameter on `destroy` and the new `workspace` parameter that callers (cloud CLI) pass into status/exec helpers — provider implementations may use it to construct `cd <workspace> && ...` commands:\n   ```python\n   class Provider(ABC):\n       def build(self, deploy_dir: Path) -> int: ...\n       def deploy(self, deploy_dir: Path, *, secrets: dict[str, str]) -> int: ...\n       def ssh_exec(self, command: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess: ...\n       def attach(self) -> int: ...\n       def logs(self, *, follow: bool = True) -> int: ...\n       def status_payload(self, *, plan: str | None, workspace: str) -> dict: ...\n       def down(self) -> int: ...\n       def destroy(self, *, volume: str | None = None) -> int: ...\n   ```\n2. **Document** in the docstring that sprint 2 will add `init_plan(self, ...)` and that providers must remain stateless beyond CLI/credential discovery.\n3. **Provide** `get_provider(name: str, spec: CloudSpec) -> Provider`. Static dict for sprint 1: `{\"railway\": RailwayProvider}`.\n\n### Step 7: Railway provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Construct** with the `RailwaySpec` (`service`, `session`, optional `project`) and stash `repo.workspace` for later. Every method targets the right service explicitly.\n2. **Implement** each ABC method as a thin wrapper around `railway` CLI:\n   - `build`: `docker build -t <image-tag> <deploy_dir>`.\n   - `deploy(deploy_dir, *, secrets)`:\n     1. If `spec.railway.project is not None`: run `railway link --project <project>` once to bind the working dir.\n     2. For each `(name, value)` in `secrets`: `railway variables --service <service> --set NAME=VALUE`.\n     3. `railway up --service <service> --detach --ci` from the deploy dir.\n   - `ssh_exec(cmd)`: `railway ssh --service <service> --session <session> -- <cmd>`.\n   - `attach`: `railway ssh --service <service> --session <session>` (interactive).\n   - `logs`: `railway logs --service <service>` (with `--follow` if requested).\n   - `status_payload(plan, workspace)`: build remote command as `cd <workspace> && megaplan status` plus `--plan <plan>` if provided. Stream stdout, parse JSON, return dict. Bare `megaplan` (globally installed); no `.venv`, no `python -m`.\n   - `down`: `railway down --service <service>`.\n   - `destroy(*, volume)`:\n     1. `railway down --service <service>`.\n     2. If `volume is not None`: `railway volume delete <volume>` (and check exit code; surface non-zero as `CliError(\"provider_failed\", stderr)` so the user sees the failure rather than silently leaving the volume around).\n3. **Detect** the `railway` binary up front via `shutil.which(\"railway\")`; if missing, raise `CliError(\"provider_unavailable\", \"Install: https://docs.railway.app/develop/cli\")`.\n4. **`deploy()` secrets**: source values from `os.environ` for each name in `spec.secrets`. If any are missing locally, raise `CliError(\"missing_secrets\", f\"Missing env vars: {missing}\")` BEFORE making any railway calls.\n5. **No retry/backoff** beyond what `railway` already does. Surface non-zero exits as `CliError(\"provider_failed\", stderr)`.\n\n## Phase 3: CLI Wiring\n\n### Step 8: Cloud CLI module (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Implement** `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` modelled on `megaplan/chain.py:528-555`. Top-level `cloud` parser with subparsers: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`. All accept an optional `--cloud-yaml PATH` (defaults to `<root>/cloud.yaml`).\n2. **Implement** `run_cloud_cli(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> int` that dispatches to per-action functions.\n3. **Per-action behaviours:**\n   - **`init`** — copies `cloud.yaml.tmpl` to `<root>/cloud.yaml`. Refuses to overwrite without `--force`.\n   - **`build`** — renders deploy dir, calls `provider.build(deploy_dir)`.\n   - **`deploy`** — renders deploy dir, gathers secret values from env, calls `provider.deploy(...)`. The provider handles `railway link --project ...` when configured.\n   - **`status`** — accepts optional `--plan NAME`; calls `provider.status_payload(plan=args.plan, workspace=spec.repo.workspace)` and prints the JSON. When `--plan` is omitted, the remote `megaplan status` is invoked with no `--plan` flag and `load_plan(root, None)` resolves the active plan server-side.\n   - **`attach`** — calls `provider.attach()`. `--session NAME` overrides the spec default for this single invocation.\n   - **`logs`** — calls `provider.logs(follow=True)`. `--no-follow` for one-shot.\n   - **`exec \"<cmd>\"`** — calls `provider.ssh_exec(cmd)`. Streams stdout/stderr; returns the remote exit code. Does NOT auto-prepend `cd <workspace>` (the user's command is opaque); document that pattern in `docs/cloud.md`.\n   - **`resume`** — accepts optional `--plan NAME`. Steps:\n     1. Call `provider.status_payload(plan=args.plan, workspace=spec.repo.workspace)`; read `next_step`.\n     2. Translate via `from megaplan.auto import _phase_command; argv = _phase_command(next_step)`. Guarantees the `execute` special case is honored.\n     3. Build the remote command: `cd <workspace> && megaplan ` + shell-quoted `argv`, plus `--plan <plan>` if the user specified one.\n     4. Hand to `provider.ssh_exec(...)`.\n   - **`down`** — calls `provider.down()`.\n   - **`destroy`** — interactive confirmation by default; `--yes` skips. On confirmation, calls `provider.destroy(volume=spec.resources.volume)`. The prompt message names the volume (or \"no volume configured\") so the user sees what's about to be deleted.\n4. **Errors:** all `CliError`s flow through `_emit_error` (mirror `megaplan/chain.py:594-597`).\n\n### Step 9: Wire into top-level CLI (`megaplan/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Register** the parser inside `build_parser()` next to the existing `build_chain_parser` registration (`megaplan/cli.py:997-998`):\n   ```python\n   from megaplan.cloud.cli import build_cloud_parser\n   build_cloud_parser(subparsers)\n   ```\n2. **Dispatch** in `main()` next to the `chain` block (`megaplan/cli.py:1133-1138`):\n   ```python\n   if args.command == \"cloud\":\n       from megaplan.cloud.cli import run_cloud_cli\n       try:\n           return run_cloud_cli(root, args)\n       except CliError as error:\n           return error_response(error, root=root)\n   ```\n3. **Do NOT** add `\"cloud\"` to `COMMAND_HANDLERS` — it has its own dispatch path.\n4. **Keep** all imports lazy/scoped.\n\n## Phase 4: Tests, Docs, Smoke Build\n\n### Step 10: Unit tests for spec & renderer (`tests/test_cloud_spec.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **`test_cloud_spec.py`** — happy-path load for each `mode`, plus rejections: missing `repo.url`, missing `repo.workspace` validation when set to a non-absolute string, unknown provider, `mode=auto` without `idea_file`, `mode=chain` without `chain.spec`, an `agents` key that isn't `default` AND isn't in `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` (assert error names the actual valid set), an unknown agent value, malformed `secrets` list, and (for `provider: railway`) a missing `railway:` block being filled with defaults (`service: agent`, `session: agent`, `project: None`).\n2. **`test_cloud_template.py`** — render with a fully-populated spec. Assert: (a) every documented placeholder is replaced (iterate over `template.PLACEHOLDERS`); (b) no original reigh hardcoded string remains (`banodoco/reigh-app`, `gpt-5.4`, `[REDACTED]`, `/workspace/reigh-app`); (c) `sandbox_mode = \"danger-full-access\"` line is preserved; (d) Claude auth block present and gated on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` with warn-not-fatal semantics; (e) the agent-routing block emits ONLY `megaplan config set agents.<step> <agent>` lines; (f) for a per-step override, exactly one `agents.<step>` line is emitted with the override agent; (g) **a non-default `repo.workspace` (e.g. `/workspace/foo`) flows into every `WORKSPACE_PATH` substitution site in the rendered entrypoint** (no `/workspace/app` literal remains).\n\n### Step 11: Unit test for the Railway provider with mocked subprocess (`tests/test_cloud_railway.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Patch** `subprocess.run` (and `shutil.which`) and assert exact `railway ...` argv produced by each provider method.\n2. **Cover** that every railway invocation includes `--service <service>` and `ssh_exec`/`attach` include `--session <session>`.\n3. **Cover** `build`, `deploy` (per-secret upload sequence with `--service`, AND when `spec.railway.project` is set the test asserts `railway link --project <project>` is called exactly once before any `variables`/`up` calls), `down`, `ssh_exec`, `attach`, `logs`.\n4. **`status_payload(plan=None, workspace=\"/workspace/foo\")`** — assert remote command is `cd /workspace/foo && megaplan status` (no `--plan`, no `.venv`, no `python -m`). `status_payload(plan=\"P\", workspace=\"/workspace/foo\")` — assert remote command is `cd /workspace/foo && megaplan status --plan P`.\n5. **`destroy(volume=None)`** — assert exactly one `railway down --service <service>` call, no volume call.\n6. **`destroy(volume=\"agent-volume\")`** — assert `railway down --service <service>` followed by `railway volume delete agent-volume`. Add a second test where the volume-delete call returns non-zero and assert it raises `CliError(\"provider_failed\", ...)`.\n7. **Cover** the missing-binary path: `shutil.which(\"railway\") -> None` ⇒ `CliError(\"provider_unavailable\", ...)`.\n8. **Cover** the missing-secret path: spec lists secret `FOO` but `os.environ` doesn't have it → `CliError` with the missing names, BEFORE any railway calls.\n\n### Step 12: Unit test for `cloud resume` translation (`tests/test_cloud_resume.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Mock** `provider.status_payload` to return `{\"next_step\": \"execute\", ...}`. Assert the cloud-resume action calls `provider.ssh_exec` with a remote command containing `cd <workspace> && megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved`.\n2. **Cover** at least one non-execute step (`next_step == \"review\"`) to verify the bare-name path also works.\n3. **Assert** that `--plan <name>` is appended when the user passed `--plan`, and omitted when they didn't.\n4. **Assert** that the remote command's `cd` target is `spec.repo.workspace` (not a hardcoded path).\n\n### Step 13: Smoke test for docker build (`tests/test_cloud_docker_build.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Skip** at module import if `shutil.which(\"docker\") is None` with a clear `pytest.skip(...)` message.\n2. **Materialise** a fully-rendered deploy dir into `tmp_path`, run `docker build -t megaplan-cloud-smoke .` against it. Fail on non-zero exit.\n3. **Mark** with `@pytest.mark.slow` so CI can opt in/out.\n\n### Step 14: User docs (`docs/cloud.md`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Create** `docs/cloud.md`. Sections: quick start, `cloud.yaml` field reference (including `repo.workspace` with the explicit note that it is the container path used for cloning, tmux cwd, and remote `cd` targets; the `railway:` block including `service`/`session`/`project` and what each one does; the `resources.volume` field with explicit note that it controls `cloud destroy` volume deletion), env vars, secret-rotation pointer, cost-monitoring pointer (Railway dashboard link), the symptom→cause→fix table ported from the reigh README.\n2. **Document** the resolved Claude non-interactive auth path (and the warn-not-fatal behaviour when no path exists).\n3. **Document** `cloud destroy` semantics: it runs `railway down --service <service>` then, if `resources.volume` is set, `railway volume delete <volume>`. Warn that without `--yes` the prompt names the volume.\n4. **Document** `cloud exec` does NOT auto-`cd` to the workspace; provide the recommended pattern `megaplan cloud exec \"cd /workspace/app && megaplan ...\"`.\n5. **Document** the `railway` CLI install link and the failure mode if it's missing.\n6. **Brief mention** that sprint 2 will add fly/ssh/local providers and `cloud init-plan`.\n\n## Execution Order\n1. Steps 0–1 first — vendoring (with ignore + build exclusion) and full read of the reigh dir feed every later decision.\n2. Steps 2–3 lay down resources before any code references them.\n3. Steps 4–5 (spec + renderer) before steps 6–7 (providers depend on spec dataclasses, including the new `repo.workspace` field).\n4. Steps 8–9 (CLI wiring) after providers exist.\n5. Tests (10–13) run alongside or after each implementation step they cover.\n6. Docs (14) last so they reflect shipped behaviour.\n\n## Validation Order\n1. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_spec.py tests/test_cloud_template.py tests/test_cloud_resume.py -x` (cheapest, no external deps).\n2. Run `pytest tests/test_cloud_railway.py -x` (mocked subprocess).\n3. Run the full suite: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -x` to confirm no regression.\n4. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud init` in a scratch dir; confirm `cloud.yaml` is written with `repo.workspace`, `railway.service`, `resources.volume` defaults.\n5. Run `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud build` against the scratch `cloud.yaml`; confirm a Docker image is produced (skip if no docker).\n6. Build the wheel (`./.venv/bin/python -m build --wheel`) and confirm `_reference/` is absent from the artifact while `templates/` and `wrappers/` are present.\n7. Manual: `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan cloud deploy` against a throwaway Railway project mirroring reigh's env vars; confirm container boots, codex auth succeeds, claude auth either succeeds or warns (never blocks), megaplan installs at the pinned ref, tmux sessions start in the configured workspace path, healthserver answers on `:8080`. `cloud status` returns parsed JSON; `cloud resume` on an `execute`-state plan dispatches `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved` over SSH; `cloud destroy --yes` runs `railway down` then `railway volume delete <name>` and the volume is gone from the Railway dashboard.",
  "changes_summary": "All 10 open flags collapse into 4 mechanical fixes; none required redesign. (1) Workspace path: added `repo.workspace: str` field (default `/workspace/app`, validated absolute), threaded through `template.PLACEHOLDERS[\"WORKSPACE_PATH\"]` (every reigh `/workspace/reigh-app` site), `Provider.status_payload(workspace=...)`, and `cloud resume`'s remote command construction. New template test asserts a non-default workspace flows end-to-end. Closes issue_hints, correctness-1, callers-1, FLAG-005. (2) Volume teardown: extended `Provider.destroy(*, volume=None)`; `RailwayProvider.destroy` runs `railway down` then `railway volume delete <volume>` when set, surfacing failures as `CliError`. CLI prompt names the volume. New tests cover both `destroy(volume=None)` and `destroy(volume=\"agent-volume\")` plus the failure path. Closes correctness-2, callers-2, FLAG-006, all_locations-1. (3) `railway.project`: now wired — `RailwayProvider.deploy` calls `railway link --project <project>` once before any `variables`/`up` calls when set; new test asserts the call sequence. No more dead knob. Closes scope. (4) `_reference/` plumbing: Step 0 explicitly appends the path to `.gitignore` and adds a `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.exclude]` entry to `pyproject.toml`; validation step 6 confirms the wheel doesn't ship it.
